#7027. Brad Pitt Wants to Play a James Bond Villain

The announcement finally arrived this week: Robert Pattinson is James Bond. But before we could even come up for air about Amazon MGM and director Denis Villeneuve’s decision to have arguably the busiest movie star in the business today follow Daniel Craig as 007, the Internet had already moved on. Fans started asking more questions. Who will costar as the next Bond girl? Who’s performing the song? And, most importantly, who would play opposite Pattinson as the villain?

Well, how about Brad Pitt? The F1 star likely didn’t expect a James Bond question when he stepped on the red carpet for the premiere of his latest film, The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth. The spinoff sequel of Quentin Tarantino’s Once Upon a Time... in Hollywood sees Pitt return as stuntman Cliff Booth under the direction of David Fincher, which couldn’t be further from the life of a British MI6 hitman. Still, Pitt was excited to hear for the first time that Pattinson landed the role when a reporter for ExtraTV shared the news. “I love this idea,” Pitt said. “He’s the real deal.”

Would he ever think about playing the villain? Pitt winked at the camera and said, “Robert? Denis? You know what, I’d like to do that. My man Javier [Bardem] did it so well [in Skyfall] It’s tall shoes, but I’d take that on.”

Later down the red carpet, Pitt answered the question again when Complex asked if he had any advice for Pattinson. “Sign me for the villain,” he said, coming around to the idea even more. “Get Denis Villeneuve on the flip phone. Let’s go!”

Elsewhere, Pattinson’s fellow Dune: Part Three costar Florence Pugh is already pitching herself as a potential villain and/or Bond girl. When Villeneuve presented her with an honor at Elle's Women in Hollywood event on Monday, Pugh congratulated the director on finding the next James Bond. “Nice catch,” she said. “Any villains the size of a five-foot-four-inch female?" I’m sure this is just the beginning.

I’ll admit, Pitt wasn’t my first choice for the next Bond villain. If anything, Pattinson was. But I love how enthusiastic he is about the possibility. Now, I kind of feel like I need to see it.

#7025. There’s Science Behind Why You’re So Obsessed With Your Partner's Smell

Is it pheromones, chemistry, or something else entirely?

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

You know that thing where your partner's T-shirt somehow smells better after they've worn it? Or when you find yourself nuzzling into their neck during a hug because you genuinely can't get enough of how they smell? It's not necessarily their cologne, shampoo, or laundry detergent you're into, either. Sometimes, it's just them. And apparently, you're really into it.

If you've ever wondered why you're so attracted to your partner's natural smell, you've probably heard that pheromones are to blame. There's this idea that our bodies can somehow sniff out the people we're most compatible with, even before our brains catch up. But the science behind that theory isn't nearly as straightforward as you might think.

“Researchers haven't definitively identified a human sex pheromone that reliably produces sexual attraction in the way we sometimes see in other species,” explains Casey Tanner, a LELO sexpert and certified sex therapist.

That doesn't mean your partner's smell has nothing to do with your feelings for them, though. Our sense of smell is closely connected to memory and emotion, which helps explain why someone's scent can become so comforting (or downright irresistible) once we're attached to them. But does loving the way your partner smells actually mean you're compatible? And should you be concerned if you don't particularly care for their scent? Here's what the science says.

Pheromones are chemical signals released by one member of a species that can trigger specific behavioral or physical responses in another member of the same species. In the animal kingdom, they play a role in everything from attracting mates to marking territory. But when it comes to humans, things get a little less clear.

According to Tanner, while pheromones are well-documented in other animals, their role in human sexual attraction remains unproven. So, there's no solid scientific evidence that your body is sending out some irresistible chemical signal that makes certain people want to jump your bones.

And while pheromones and body odor often get lumped together, they're not actually the same thing. According to Tanner, your natural body odor is essentially your unique scent signature, shaped by everything from genetics and hormones to skin bacteria, diet, and your environment. Unlike pheromones, which are defined by the biological responses they trigger, ordinary body odor doesn't have to produce a specific response to influence how someone feels about you. So, while you might find someone's body odor incredibly attractive, that doesn't mean you're responding to pheromones.

Research suggests that body odor can influence attraction and who we're drawn to, but scientists are still figuring out exactly how much of a role it plays. And just because someone's natural scent appeals to you doesn't mean your bodies have identified each other as a perfect genetic match.

If you've ever found yourself lingering over your partner's pillow after they've left, there's a reason their scent can feel so comforting. And it has a lot to do with how closely our sense of smell is connected to memory and emotion.

“When we're falling for someone, their smell can become intertwined with experiences of pleasure, affection, comfort, and arousal,” Tanner explains. “Over time, that scent can become a kind of shortcut to feeling close to that person!”

Basically, your brain starts associating your partner's natural scent with all the things you love about being around them, from cuddling on the couch to having sex. And those associations can stick around even when you're physically apart.

“Once we’re attached to that person, their body odor essentially becomes a reinforcement or reward,” says psychologist Pamela Dalton, PhD, MPH. Their scent can bring back positive memories and feelings of intimacy, which helps explain why something as simple as a worn shirt can make you feel closer to someone you're missing.

As much as we'd love to tell you that being obsessed with your partner's natural scent means you've found your soulmate, science doesn't exactly back that up. Sure, there may be some biological factors at play, but liking the way someone smells isn't proof that you're genetically compatible, much less destined to spend your lives together.

Some research has explored whether people are more attracted to the body odor of potential partners whose immune-system genes differ from their own. The theory is that these differences could lead to greater immune diversity in biological offspring. But according to Tanner, the findings have been inconsistent, and there's no reliable evidence that humans choose romantic partners based on genetic compatibility.

And even if someone's scent makes you want to bury your face in their chest 24/7, that doesn't tell you much about whether you'll actually work as a couple.

“Loving your partner's scent can be a true reflection of attraction and attachment, but no, it’s not a reliable diagnostic tool for long-term compatibility,” Tanner says. “Chemistry is a great indicator of initial attraction, but compatibility is something we have to discover, negotiate, and create over time.”

In other words, enjoying someone's natural smell is a nice bonus, but it's not a substitute for shared values, trust, communication, or the other things that make a relationship work. And if you're not immediately drawn to someone's scent, that doesn't mean attraction can't develop as you get to know them.

If you've made it this far and realized you've never once felt the urge to steal your partner's T-shirt just to smell it, don't stress. Not everyone is particularly sensitive to scent, and being indifferent to your partner's natural smell doesn't mean you're any less attracted to them.

According to Tanner, people vary in how sensitive they are to smells, how much attention they pay to them, and how important scent is to their experience of attraction. Some people might be more drawn to their partner's appearance, voice, personality, or the emotional connection they share.

“If you’re indifferent to a person’s smell, it’s likely not going to be a game-changer because once you have the opportunity to get to know the person, you find [other] positives and compatibility between the two of you,” says Dalton.

And if you're otherwise happy with your relationship, there's no reason to start questioning it just because your partner's scent doesn't do anything special for you. As Tanner puts it, “If you’re into them, don’t go looking for a problem just because you haven’t noticed their scent!”

Okay, but what if you used to love your partner's natural scent and now you can't stand it? Before you start questioning your entire relationship, know that a change in how you perceive someone's smell doesn't automatically mean your feelings for them have changed.

According to Tanner, everything from hormonal fluctuations and medications to diet, illness, and changes in your sense of smell can affect how you experience your partner's scent. Pregnancy, for example, can make you more sensitive to certain smells. And sometimes, the explanation is as simple as your partner switching shampoos or laundry detergents.

Of course, emotions can play a role, too. Because scent is so closely connected to memory and feelings, your associations with someone's smell can change as your relationship evolves.

“There is evidence that when, for whatever reason, that relationship isn’t working, that body odor is no longer pleasant or preferred,” says Dalton. “All of a sudden, because of the association with negative characteristics, [the body odor] is changing. Your response is changing.”

Still, suddenly disliking your partner's scent isn't proof that something is wrong between you. As Tanner explains, a change in smell isn't a reliable indicator of relationship conflict, and there may be a physical explanation worth considering first.

If you notice a significant change in your partner's actual body odor, it's worth gently asking whether anything has changed with their health, medications, or routine. And if there's a particularly noticeable change in genital odor, Tanner recommends encouraging them to check in with a healthcare professional.

Bottom line: A change in how your partner smells to you isn't automatically a sign that something's wrong. How you feel about your partner's scent can change for all sorts of reasons, and it's just one small part of the bigger picture.

#7023. To No One’s Surprise, Trump’s Handpicked Navy Secretary Is Just as Incompetent and Heartless as He Is

Hung Cao’s confirmation hearing went just about as badly as any I’ve ever seen.

Maybe, like me, you had a bad day on Tuesday. But I assure you that nobody on this planet had a worse day on Tuesday than Hung Cao, the acting secretary of the Navy who met the Senate Armed Services Committee on Tuesday in pursuit of being confirmed in that position. He left the hearing, as the old boxing writers used to put it, in a blanket.

Cao tried to talk his way around the wretched treatment of sailors on the USS Abraham Lincoln during that vessel’s extended deployment in support of whatever the hell we’re doing in the Persian Gulf. It did not go well. Some highlights below.

Here’s Senator Mark Kelly after Cao implied that sailors on the Lincoln lost weight because they were picky eaters:

(Personally, I wanted to hear more about Aunt Pearl, the nurse with the gun in her bra. My father’s Liberty ship brought some of those German POWs back over here. Maybe he knew Aunt Pearl, who didn’t shoot him.)

The whole thing was a theater of the absurd. Secretary Cao first came to public notice when, as a Senate candidate in Virginia, he surprised the nation with the news that witches had taken over California. This was worse.

#7022. 13 Clever Details I Saw at the 2026 Kips Bay Decorator Show House in Dallas

Check out the first ever home gym in Kips Bay Show House history.

The seventh annual Kips Bay Decorator Show House in Dallas returns to 2999 Turtle Creek Boulevard, but don’t expect it to look anything like it did in 2025. A new group of the country’s leading designers has taken on the 25,000-square-foot French Renaissance estate, transforming each room to showcase bold ideas, highlight exceptional craftsmanship, and reveal the upcoming trends we can expect to see in the world of interior design.

Visit the show house and see each of the 22 designers’ rooms, from the first dedicated home gym in Kips Bay history by Dallas-based Platinum Gym Design, to a small but mighty European-inspired kitchen by Stephenson Home, to a 1970s-inspired primary bathroom fit with a glass brick shower and curtained-off water closet by Nuance Interiors. With sponsors like Benjamin Moore, The Shade Store, The Rug Company, and more, the vibrant and personality-filled spaces come as no surprise.

As always, proceeds from the show house go to the Kips Bay Boys & Girls Club, which provides educational, enrichment, and developmental programs to thousands of young people, as well as Dallas-based nonprofits Dwell with Dignity and The Crystal Charity Ball.

To see this celebration of exceptional design, you can purchase tickets here and visit the house from Saturday, November 14th to Sunday, December 6th. However, if you can’t make it to the Lone Star State in time, check out some of our favorite moments from the event below.

Kelsi Lamberth of Nuance Interiors pulled inspiration from the 1970s for her primary bathroom, separating the shower from the main prep space using a curved glass brick wall. The sculptural aspects can be found throughout in this groovy space.

In the primary bedroom, Troy Rivington of Rivington Marx Interiors cocooned the room in a dark floral wallpaper and accessorized with other moody botanicals throughout. He was inspired by the romance of 1940s Cairo, dressing the room up like it belonged to a well-traveled collector.

As the first ever dedicated home gym at the Kips Bay Show House, Peter Hoffman of Platinum Gym Design chose a refined, luxurious setup. He named the room Tanren after the Japanese art of hammering and refining metal, and used walnut in both the equipment and the ceiling slats, stainless steel frames, and brown leather accents to complete the exercise space.

In this bespoke bathroom, Russ Peters and Bill Cates of Peters Cates Design lined the wall behind the Studio McGee x Kohler bathtub with a rich merlot tile by RIAD Tile. It’s a beautiful statement and a fabulous way to bring more texture and dimension to the room.

In this tucked away library space, filled with collected finds, designers Christina Garcia Lysaught and Grayson Knight of Layered Dimensions finished off this dark space with a stunning statement mirror. Around the perimeter are resin busts of wildlife, like owls and deer, bringing in a bejeweled touch to the room.

The small but mighty kitchen, designed by Katie Stephenson of Stephenson Home, is crowned with a crackled-gold blooming wallpaper, bringing a warm balance to the rest of the room. It’s covered in birds, butterflies, and blossoms, and it wraps the cook space in a golden glow, reminding all who enter that this room should be as beautiful as it is functional.

The citron paint color wrapping the walls in Jen Bienvenu’s entrance hall is undoubtedly enchanting and serves as the perfect backdrop for her “After the Hunt” theme, but it’s the lampshades made from old silk saris that tie everything together for us. They provide such a magical warmth and really capture the essence of a collector’s treasure room.

Related Story: Almost Every Piece in This Layered Arkansas Home Was a Lucky Antique Find

Step through the citron entry and enter the round room, designed in collaboration by Helen Bergin Interiors and Lichten Architects. The walls are softened by long curtains from The Shade Store, which create beautiful juxtaposition with the curved, sculptural silver media console by Asher Gillman. The actual turntable is flush to the surface, giving it a seamless look while it emits wonderful sound.

In the Withdrawing Room by Jen Stevens of Fonde Interiors, plaster botanicals by artist Amanda Norman come to life as they crawl from the mantel along the chimney breast, escaping to the ceiling. The vines were handmade by Norman and capture the essence of a garden left undisturbed, showing that this formal-at-first-glance room has a wilder side.

The rosy palette in designer Jen Dean’s—founder of Jede Interiors—grand bathroom is topped off with a hand-painted floral mural that covers the ceiling. The rose-gold shine reflects the natural light in the room and give it more depth, while the natural motifs spread across the rest of the space.

Inside this observatory bedroom by sisters Amy Mobley and Katie Norrid of City Hill Design, the Verellen bed is raised up from the ground on a custom platform, emphasizing the importance of the furniture’s role in this space meant for relaxation and rest. It’s wrapped in a striped silk carpet that beckons your touch with a chartreuse mohair sofa at the foot.

Related Story: A Trip to the Lone Star State Inspired the Earthy Textures of This Tennessee New Build

Colorful and collected, this vibrant library by designer Betsy Wentz is not visually quiet. The maximalist space is covered in pattern-mixed Schumacher from ceiling to floor, and the designer pumped up the volume by opting for this triangular table in this tucked away corner.

In designer Amy Switzer’s textural bar lounge—filled with plush chairs, unique artwork, large tassel accessories, and beaded cigarettes—it’s the stone details that really make the room sing. Onyx cornices crown the windows, blue agate inlays decorate the floor, and a banquette which curves around a custom stone and wood table bring a natural coolness to the room that only stone can bring.

#7021. Taylor Swift Just Wore the Burgundy Manicure and Pedicure I Want All Season Long

It’s perfect for the transition from fall to winter.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Last night, Taylor Swift made a glamorous appearance at the CMAs, stunning us not only with a performance that mashed up “I Knew It, I Knew You” and “Our Song,” but also with her red-carpet look. Her hair was pulled back into an effortless updo, she traded her beloved bold red lip for a warmer-toned shade, and she topped it all off with a glossy burgundy manicure.

The deep red is pretty much the perfect late-fall nail color. It feels festive enough for Thanksgiving and will carry you straight through the holiday season. Plus, it's also an easy shade to wear, no matter your nail shape and length. Swift opted for short, softly rounded square nails, but the color would look just as elevated on a longer almond or oval shape, too. She even carried the hue through to her toes, opting for a matching pedicure to complete the look.

It's a shade that goes with practically anything in your closet. Swift wore hers with a bright yellow gown, but on the off chance that you don’t have a red-carpet event coming up (stars, they’re just like us!) you can easily wear the glossy burgundy with a cozy knit sweater or your favorite winter coat. It's a fall classic for a reason.

And while Swift tends to keep her go-to beauty products under wraps (we can only speculate about her favorite cat-eye liner or the exact red lipstick tubes in her rotation), we don't know which polish she wore. Still, there are plenty of gorgeous deep red shades to choose from. I've rounded up some of my favorites below, all of which are perfect for a simple DIY manicure.

#7018. These MacBook Deals Are Good Enough to Shop Before Black Friday

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Considering the price of tech, we’ll gladly take $250 off a new MacBook.

If there’s one Apple product line where the recent price hikes hit the hardest, it’s the MacBook. Rising RAM and storage costs have pushed laptop prices up across the board, which makes scoring a new MacBook on sale all the more mission critical.

As luck would have it, Black Friday deals on Apple tech are already ramping up on Amazon, including discounts on MacBooks. While there’s always the chance these deals might continue to deepen in the next week, the current discounts are nothing to sneeze at if you can’t wait that long to shop.

Of course, not all MacBooks were created equal. While the standard MacBook Air is great for most, those who are planning on running hardcore software might be benefit from a beefed-up MacBook Pro. And if budget is your primary concern, we’re of the opinion that the MacBook Neo is a worthy buy, even if it’s only $50 off right now.

The best MacBook Air deal you’ll find right now is this $250 discount on the 15-inch model. With this laptop, you’ll get Apple’s M5 chip in a fanless, 3.3-pound frame that handles heavy multitasking and light video editing without breaking a sweat. Base storage doubled to 512 GB this year—a great upgrade for storing more apps, photos, and files—and the 15.3-inch Liquid Retina display is sharp and vivid. You also get a 12 MP Center Stage camera that’s great on video calls, WiFi 7 for fast downloads, and up to 18 hours of battery, enough for a busy workday and then some. It won’t replace a MacBook Pro for serious creative work or gaming, but for school, business, and general use, it’s the one to get.

Deals on 13-inch models aren’t as steep—yet. But we expect that to change in a week’s time.

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 15-inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 13-inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 512 GB)

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 13-Inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Given that Apple just dropped a new slate of M6 MacBook Pros, in addition to the company’s first-ever touchscreen laptop, we were anticipating discounts on the M5 range to clear out inventory. In the lead-up to Black Friday, you can shave $200 off of the 14-inch model, complete with a Liquid Retina XDR display with 120Hz ProMotion, up to 24 hours of battery, three Thunderbolt 4 ports, HDMI, an SD card slot, and up to 2 TB of storage.

Why go Pro over the Air? The GPU is fast, which makes a difference when editing video, working with high-res photos, or gaming, and the XDR screen is significantly brighter. If your daily workload has started becoming too much for the Air to handle, this is where you should look next. If you want the biggest, most powerful model possible, souped-up MacBook Pros are up to $500 off.

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 16-Inch (M5 Max, 36 GB RAM, 2 TB)

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 14-Inch (M5 Pro, 24 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 16-Inch (M5 Pro, 24 GB RAM, 2 TB)

The MacBook Neo is Apple’s most affordable laptop, even with the recent price hike from $599 to $699. Right now, the Neo is modestly discounted, although we’re holding out hope that it might drop down to its inaugural price—or even lower—once Black Friday proper arrives. The Neo is an easy recommendation for anyone looking for their first Mac. It runs on Apple’s A18 Pro chip and is surprisingly quick for browsing, sending email, and streaming video. The 13-inch Liquid Retina display looks great for the price, and the battery lasts a solid 16 hours. It also comes in four fun colors, with the yellow-green citrus being a fan favorite.

There are some trade-offs with going with Apple’s budget model, including storage that starts at 256 GB, just 8 GB of RAM, and no keyboard backlight. You also get only two USB-C ports, so getting a USB-C hub is a must if you plan to connect a lot of accessories. Spending an extra $100 gets you a Neo with 512 GB of storage and a Touch ID sensor for quick unlocking. For students or anyone who mostly sends emails and browses the Web, the Neo is hard to beat.

#7017. Hailey Bieber and Bella Hadid Make a Case For “Coyote Chai” Colored Fur Coats

Chocolate brown’s cooler cousin has officially entered the chat.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Move over, chocolate brown: There's a new shade taking over the celebrity style scene, and it's honestly the perfect shade for transitioning from fall to winter. Enter what I’m coining Coyote Chai, the deliciously rich, multi-tonal brown fur coat trend that Bella Hadid and Hailey Bieber are making a serious case for right now.

If winter dressing had a moodboard, these two would be running it. And while their personal aesthetics might differ, they've seemingly landed on the same outerwear obsession: fluffy, statement-making coats in a gorgeous mix of earthy browns, caramel, taupe, and deep brown.

Bella stepped out in a fuzzy, sandy-brown jacket paired with dark-wash denim and heels, alongside a brown Coach purse. Meanwhile, Hailey took a similarly tonal approach in New York, wearing a cropped, textured fur-effect jacket with warm brown, honey, and gray undertones, styled with classic jeans and barely-there black heels.

While it feels like chocolate brown has been the star of the show, this shift into what I’m calling Coyote Chai feels like the rightful move as we head closer to winter, brightening the sartorial vibes as it gets gloomier outside. This shade of brown is the cooler, blended cousin of espresso and caramel shades. As a non-coffee drinker and chai latte warrior, this light brown shade blended with hints of darker spots is the perfect layering style coat to be bundled up in. And as Bieber and Hadid show, the coat makes the simplest of outfits all the more interesting.

Consider this your sign to grab a good coat for winter, and maybe make it in this fun Coyote Chai shade.

#7015. The 63 Best New Perfumes and Fragrances of 2026

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

We sniffed, we spritzed, we sprayed. Only the perfumes below left a lingering impression, earning them a spot on our annual best-of list.

Everything smells like vanilla. At least, that’s what perfume naysayers will tell you. And it’s somewhat true: If you’re judging by what’s popular, ubiquitous, or cool with the youth, then sure—vanilla scents and sweet gourmands are still trending.

But hundreds of other fragrance launches in 2026 deserve a spot on your vanity and a spritz on your skin, and many are far less sweet. Gourmands took a turn for the weird and fruity this year, with notes like lychee and rice milk popping up in otherwise innocuous formulas. Woods and ambers continued to reign, as did spice-forward and citrus scents with unexpected depth. Perfumers are meeting our demand for newness in increasingly interesting, artistic ways, and we smelled hundreds of their creations to choose the 63 winners of our annual fragrance awards.

See all the winners of the 2026 Harper’s Bazaar Fragrance Awards, plus quotes from our beauty editors on what makes these formulas worthy of your next blind buy.

“Perfumer Olivier Polge reinvented the beloved “Coco Mad” scent as Crush Absolu, a new juice centered around patchouli, amber, rose, and a lychee-grapefruit accord. ‘There’s something voluptuous, something very prideful, something very addictive,’ Polge says about the new scent.”

“The new iteration of J’adore was inspired by Rihanna herself. As such, it’s a sweeter, bolder take, with notes of honeyed jasmine, rose, ylang-ylang, and vanilla.” —JR

"Bazaar’s Beauty Commerce Editor, Tiffany Dodson Davis, describes Guerlain’s L’Heure Bleue Eau de Parfum as the “scent of sunset,” thanks to a blue-sky accord that evokes the moment the sun goes down and the air turns crisp, clean, and fresh." —KI

Burberry Goddess Amber Vanilla Eau de Parfum Intense

“Lavender, maple syrup, four vanilla notes—I feel like a dog hearing all my favorite words. The result is a warm, ambery gourmand perfume that’s perfect for the cooler months.” —JR

Armani Beauty Emporio Armani Power of You Eau de Parfum

"Passionfruit adds an unexpected zing to the vanilla base here, making Power of You one of our favorite tropical-leaning gourmands of the year. It's pure summertime sex appeal." —JR

“Henry Rose Ripe captures fruits—from lychee to watermelon—at their juiciest, ripest point, like a delectable first bite, before packing a punch with black pepper, cardamom, and powdery florals.” —Katie Intner, beauty market and commerce editor

“One of the most unexpected fruity florals on the list, Influxious Magic Hour melds watermelon and dragon fruit with spicy chili pepper for a burst of juicy energy.” —KI

Balmain Paris Destin De Balmain Eau de Parfum

"The opening note of Destin is strawberry, a modern and sweet fruity note that quickly dries down to a patchouli-forward, gorgeous floral musk. It will make you smell expensive." —JR

"Neroli, bergamot, and yuzu combine with notes of orange blossom, jasmine, and tonka bean for a well-rounded fragrance that stands out from the pack. Parfum de Marly's Athénaïs eau de parfum has a zesty composition upon first spritz before drying down to a powdery finish." —Tiffany Dodson Davis, beauty commerce editor

“Gap recently brought back its iconic fragrance line from the ’90s, including the nostalgic Grass Eau de Parfum. It now features matcha, vetiver, green mandarin, and cedar for a fresh take on its outdoorsy, earthy scent.” —KI

Maison Francis Kurkdjian Oud Velvet Mood Extrait de Parfum

“For those who are oud-averse, Maison Francis Kurkdjian’s Oud Velvet Mood is a lighter iteration of the category. The oud has a woody quality, and when paired with spicy cinnamon, rich saffron, and juicy peach, the result is light and warm like the sun.” —KI

“This stunning perfume is all gardenia and vanilla, and in its simplicity, it happens to be one of our most complimented scents of the year.” —JR

"This is a bold, long-wearing scent for those who live and die for the sensual pairing of rose and oud. We're obsessed with the way it blooms and evolves on skin throughout the night." —JR

“Ex Nihilo Demon Dancer feels like moving through the crisp air on a spring day, with peppermint providing a breezy contrast to juicy rhubarb, bright bergamot, and warm tonka bean.” —KI

“We don’t know what we love more: the magnetic floral perfume that begs to be worn every day, the sculptural bottle, or the bookish packaging that made us a fan before we even took our first whiff.” —JR

“You can’t go wrong with Beyoncé’s sultry and sophisticated hair perfume, which leaves hair with a long-wearing trail of bergamot, jasmine, oud, sandalwood, and Haitian vetiver.” —JR

“Delightfully unisex, this aromatic scent is a masterclass in balance. The top opens with bergamot, cardamom, pink pepper, and wormwood. In the middle, you’ll find fruity fig, lavender, and violet leaf. The base is iris, amberwood, and ambrette.” —JR

“Earthy and green, with an olive top note and banana base note—this is the perfume to buy this year if you want to smell wholly original and unabashedly cool.” —JR

“Creamy and warm with notes of chocolate and coffee, this is the must-have gourmand perfume of the winter. In a word, it makes us feel happy.” —JR

Discothèque Next to Me or Nothing Eau De Parfum

“More of a feeling than a fragrance, this scent is airy and easy to wear, with breezy notes of milk, jasmine, and woods.” —JR

"Gourmand lovers will want to overindulge in this elevated eau de parfum, which combines two types of creams—whipped and coconut—along with jasmine blossom and vanilla mousse notes for a sophisticated yet playful olfactory blend." —TDD

#7012. A Full Timeline of Ella Langley and Tucker Wetmore’s Rumored Romance, Because It’s Very Confusing

If you’re a country music fan (or tbh, even if you’re not), then you’re probably well aware that the love lives of country music’s rising stars are quite entangled…and have been for a minute now. The latest (reported) update? Ella Langley and Tucker Wetmore are rumored to be dating, but we really can’t talk about their maybe-relationship without discussing Tucker’s ex Bryana Ferringer...or Ella’s rumored ex Riley Green (who went on to maybe date Bryana).

Ella and Tucker may have sparked dating rumors late last year, but it wasn’t without some drama on the way. It’s a pretty confusing ride, so buckle up and let’s get into it. Starting with…

After months of dating rumors following the success of Ella and Riley’s song “You Look Like You Love Me,” Ella finally shuts down Riley dating rumors. “We’re not dating,” she tells Taste of Country, adding, “We’re just good friends.”

Riley also shuts down dating speculation during an appearance on The Viall Files podcast. “Ella’s probably too smart to date me anyway,” he jokes, later adding, “When we first started having the success we did with that song, I kinda told Ella, I was like, ‘There’s gonna be a lot of things said, but I mean, you don’t really have to play into it or not play into it.’”

Tucker posts a TikTok on August 20 with the caption “@ my ex,” sparking breakup rumors with his model girlfriend Bryana (who, btw, appeared in his music video for “Wind Up Missin’ You”).

Soon after, Bryana is spotted with Riley at his Red Rocks tour stop.

Ella and Tucker both attend the 2025 BMI Country Awards on November 18, though it’s unclear whether this is the first time they ever meet (or if they even interacted, despite standing right next to each other in this group photo):

A day later, after several reported sightings around Nashville, Riley brings Bryana as his date to the CMA Awards on November 19.

However! Riley later refers to Bryana as merely a “friend,” telling Backstage Country, “It’s gonna be tough to tiptoe out of having a date to an award show. Yeah, I mean, you go to award shows all the time, and I’m always by myself, or I’ve got, like, a security guy, or a buddy with me, so I took a friend with me to the award show.”

Around the same time, Ella sparks dating rumors with Farmer Wants a Wife season 3 star John Sansone (whose cousin, music exec Sophia Sansone, previously dated Riley). Rumors begin after John includes a picture of Ella’s dog, Crue, in a November 26 Instagram photo dump...and then, on the same day, Ella includes a video of herself shooting shotguns with John in a photo dump of her own.

John later confirms to Taste of Country in April 2026 that he and Ella “dated for a couple of months” after he slid into her DMs but are no longer together.

Oh, and side note: John’s ex from Farmer Wants a Wife, Samantha Riehl, posted a TikTok with Tucker back in April 2025 with the caption, “Blondes are the worst.”

And then right after, John posted a TikTok with Bryana at Stagecoach (which has since been deleted).

Pretty sure nothing ever happened between Samantha and Tucker (or between Bryana and John), but still, it feels worth mentioning!!

On December 5, Tucker releases “Proving Me Right,” which fans are preeeetty convinced is about his ex, Bryana.

A few weeks later, Ella and Tucker are photographed together in the British Virgin Islands at the Soggy Dollar Bar...but considering the photo also includes other country artists, rumors circulate that the trip was possibly a writing retreat.

Tucker and Ella reunite to sing a live acoustic duet of Tucker’s “Sunburn” at the Heart Strings for Hope charity event on March 10.

And while Tucker didn’t write “Sunburn” himself, a few of the lyrics kiiiinda seem to allude to his December getaway with Ella.

Tucker then appears on the March 17 episode of Kristin Cavallari’s Let’s Be Honest podcast, where he claims that he has “never been broken up with” and has always been the one to “call it quits.” When Kristin asks if he has “sworn off blondes,” Tucker says, “Yep. I have. For the foreseeable future. That’s right.”

And then, a few days after that on March 21, Tucker posts an Instagram photo of himself holding a fish, and Ella slides into his comments with, “Cute fish.” Flirty!

Tucker discusses his hit “Brunette” with Audacy, saying, “My mom’s always said, ‘You're going to mess around with blondes for a little while but I truly believe you're going to settle down with the brunette,’ and I was like, ‘Yeah, 100 percent.’”

Not long after, during an appearance on Taste of Country Nights, Tucker notes, “Pretty much every girlfriend I have ever had was blonde, and I think it’s time to change up a couple of things.”

On September 23 (just a day after Ella breaks Mariah Carey’s record for longest-running Billboard Hot 100 hit with “Choosin’ Texas”), TMZ breaks the news that Tucker and Ella are (reportedly) dating.

The outlet obtains photos of the two out with friends in Nashville on the night of September 22 following the Nashville Songwriter Awards, with sources telling TMZ that the two met “through the music industry and mutual friends.”

Multiple sources also confirm the rumored romance to People so…it seems legit to me!

While making an appearance at WAVE in Wichita, Kansas, a fan straight-up asks Tucker whether he and Ella “are a thing.”

Tucker’s two-word response? “Next question.” Okay!!

Ella and Tucker seemingly take another trip to the Virgin Islands…at least, according to a sneaky fan who spotted them entering a restaurant together.

Soon after, they attend the CMAs, where they walk the carpet separately but still can’t dodge questions about their relationship status. When asked by People if there is a certain brunette he’s rooting for, he jokingly answers, “Zach Top or Stephen Wilson Jr.” Mmmkay!

Ella is also caught grinning during Tucker’s performance, which...isn’t confirmation of anything but certainly telling.

#7010. As More Wins for Democrats Roll In, We Must Remind Ourselves That the Fight Is Still Ahead

Alaska was finally called for Mary Peltola. I sure hope Hakeem Jeffries, the soon-to-be Speaker of the House, knows what to do with his majority.

I rather like these extended post-election activities. Every few days, another seat in Congress flips from R to D, and all the joy and wonder comes flooding back again. Up in Alaska, Democratic candidate Mary Peltola was (finally) declared the winner of the Senate race. She also managed to clear the 50 percent mark, which means that the race didn’t have to go into the land of ranked-choice voting, which would have resulted in Republicans birthing bovines all over Alaska and the Lower 48.

For the record, Peltola’s total votes exceeded those of both Dan Sullivans combined. Whoever deployed the JFK strategy failed against the power of fish, family, and freedom. And, truth be told, Peltola, thus far, has been a committed Blue Dog. In her one term in the House, she was one of the strongest Democratic supporters of some conservative immigration policies. She also lined up against Washington, D.C.’s independence in sentencing criminals. But, big tent and all of that.

Meanwhile, in the national capital, Representative Hakeem Jeffries easily won the endorsement of the Democratic caucus to be the next Speaker. It was particularly easy because there were no other candidates. From The New York Times:

The question remains open as to whether Jeffries—and, to a more critical extent, Senator Chuck Schumer—recognize the power they’ve been given by the midterm elections, a necessary precondition to putting that power to actual use. The iron will never be hotter.

Hey, it’s not called the biggest sale of the year for nothing!

If Black Friday isn’t technically here until November 27, why bother shopping now? I’m so glad you asked! Plus, you can get your shopping done early without risking a coveted item selling out. I'm obsessed with the digital camera resurgence. Not only does it take me back to the days of snapping photos at middle school dances, but I love how this simple point-and-shoot device gets me off of my phone when I'm spending quality time with friends and family. A must-buy, especially when it's on sale.

#7000. A Roundtable of Men in Their Nineties Taught Me the Secrets of Longevity

Every single thing I learned is something you can start doing right now.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Alan Patricof, 92, is one of my idols. The legendary venture capitalist was an early investor in enterprises as varied as AOL, Apple, and New York magazine. More important to me, aside from his incredible career and philanthropic work, he is a man who believes in always moving forward into the future. He co-launched a fund called Primetime Partners when he was 85. At 88, he wrote his first book, No Red Lights: Reflections on Life, 50 Years in Venture Capital and Never Driving Alone. He was the oldest participant—and finisher—in the New York City Marathon at 88 and got remarried at 89 to the globally recognized art advisor Barbara Guggenheim.

So when Patricof—who has also become one of my friends—called me with a request to join in on a lunch he was hosting for 12 men in their 90s, I was intrigued. I have been reporting on longevity for several years now and lead a company called ROARforward focused on the subject. Patricof asked me to come and talk about what I had learned about longevity and the possibility of the 100-year life. I agreed, but I immediately thought of it differently—as a master class in wisdom and experience where I stood to learn at least as much from these men as they could learn from me.

When I saw the list of guests, it was a bit intimidating. It included a former CEO of a Fortune 100 company, a vice chairman emeritus at one of the largest banks on the planet, a major real estate developer, and a man who has his name on one of the most prestigious health systems in New York. And those are just the ones who agreed to be identified—some asked to remain anonymous.

You get the idea: This was not an everyday group of 90-somethings but rather a gathering of highly accomplished, successful men who continue to thrive in a variety of industries and disciplines. That only made me more interested, though I imagined it might take some work to translate their habits and the ways they were spending their time into things more accessible to everyday people.

Luckily, I would turn out to be wrong about that.

As the men assembled in a private room at Patroon, a popular Manhattan restaurant, I saw that many of them already knew each other, some from as far back as grade school. As Patricof went over ground rules, including no talk of politics (both parties were represented) and no talk of sex (though Viagra did find its way into the conversation), they moved around the room, shaking hands, asking about wives and kids, and explaining what was keeping them busy.

That was my first clue that the behaviors that helped these men stay robust into their tenth decade had little to do with wealth. Research has shown that cultivating deep, healthy relationships is one of the best things you can do to increase your chances of having a long, healthy life. These men were living examples of what that looks like, and it’s free. (Though not if you have your lunches at Patroon.)

These men took care of three major aspects of their lives: health, head, and heart. First, they were committed to a fitness regimen and healthy eating habits. Second, they were highly engaged and curious individuals who valued a strong sense of purpose. Third, each of them had deep relationships with loved ones, whether a spouse, family, or friends.

You may notice that these are all universal virtues. They’re also things that can give you a sense of fulfillment if you’re only in your 80s, or 70s, or even younger. They aren’t solely investments in the future. You do them now, and they benefit you now. Which is a good feeling and precisely what makes longevity worth it in the first place. As one of the men said, “If today is the last day of my life, I’ve had a helluva run.”

“At 90, I tried boxing at a gym near my home in Harlem,” said George Hirsch, 92, before adding, proudly, that he went a full round. Hirsch was the longtime chairman of the New York Roadrunners running club. While he is no longer out doing road races, he is on a treadmill four or five days a week in a walk-run regimen. He supplements that with rowing, the elliptical machine, weights, and, apparently, the occasional prizefight, in a 60-minute routine.

For a gathering men who were 90 or older, this was a remarkably healthy group. It’s true they all have had access to the best health care, but none had problems with issues that come for everyone, like cognitive decline, diabetes, and heart disease. That word routine seemed to be the key. Routine, regimen—take your pick. These men make a point of committing to physical activity rather than just dabbling in it. One told me, “I think the secret to my life physically is that I’ve worked with the same trainer three days a week for 29 years,” adding that “even when I travel, I take my iPad and work out with him wherever I am.”

In some cases, these routines would be the envy of any younger man! The men had all played some type of sports when they were younger and carried that behavior deep into their adult lives. Since movement and exercise are the top natural medicines for a long, healthy life, these guys were on it. Activities included walking, weight training, stationary bicycling, swimming, pickleball, tennis, and downhill skiing. Two of them have a regular tennis game, and one of the oldest in the group is still an avid golfer. Most of them go to a gym or have a personal trainer. While a few admitted that they were slower as they got older—a sign of remarkable maturity in a man, to admit that—they were all active.

They are also conscious of what they eat. They agreed that they consume less food than in the past and drink very little, if at all. As Hirsch said, “I never drank much, maybe a beer or glass of wine with a meal. But I don’t drink anymore—it just doesn’t mean that much to me.”

Their attention to eating habits stood out. One thing that especially interested them was GLP-1’s, because of their impact on diet but also other potential benefits. As I shared what I’ve learned about longevity, including the frontiers of research on the topic, they asked question after question about the emergence of GLP-1’s and other classes of drugs that may enhance individual longevity. That kind of constant, engaged questioning was a hallmark of the group.

Only one of the men at the lunch defined himself as “retired.” The others said they were, at most, “semiretired.” While they are not necessarily working full throttle in what was their main career, to a man they all have very busy days—and that busyness, I found, is driven by a voracious curiosity, which is one of the best ways to strengthen and maintain brain health.

Ken Langone is a great example. He came from a working-class family in Queens and is still in awe of his good fortune. He’s taken it to mean that, as long as he’s able, he should be doing everything he can not to squander what he has. “I won big, and I can’t tell you how grateful I am,” he said, adding, “We should be giving, not taking.”

He is in his office three days a week and works from home two days a week, engaged in a public board, three private boards, his family office, and other investments. His commitment to public health is incredibly important to him; he serves as chair emeritus of NYU Langone Health.

“At 91,” Langone says, “my biggest issue is boredom, sitting around with nothing to do.”

Jim Freund, also 91, was a high-powered attorney into his early 60s, did a stint as a mediator after that, and now has a robust life as a writer, photographer, and musician.

He’s written 20 fiction and nonfiction books and has produced 25 albums of music featuring him at the piano. For six years, he has circulated a monthly blog for more than 500 friends and colleagues that includes the details of various projects he’s working on—an essay or short fiction, a piece of music, a portfolio of photos.

One expression of this curiosity is that all the men want to keep current. They follow the news, are dedicated readers, and have a deep curiosity about emerging trends. They don’t just read print; they’re on their phones, Kindles, Audible—anywhere there’s action. And AI—they’re on ChatGPT like everyone else. Some even have digital assistants.

But being current doesn’t just mean trendspotting. It can also mean having hobbies and projects that force you to engage with the world as it is. All of these men had ambitious new endeavors in the works.

Hirsch now has a bimonthly podcast with the running legend Amby Burfoot called “Running: State of the Sport.” Dan Rose, 97, former president and chairman of Rose Associates, the company that built Pentagon City and One Financial Center in Boston, is writing a new book. Patricof and his wife are taking Yiddish lessons. At least one man reported that he was finally learning the piano.

For all of them, it is important that their activities, whatever they may be, connect to deeper meaning and purpose.

Larry Leeds, 97, the former CEO and chairman of the apparel company Manhattan Industries, now works at Buckingham Capital Management five days a week buying and selling securities. It allows him to keep up with trends and investments. But it’s far from the most important thing to which he’s dedicated his energy.

Leeds hopes the leading line in his obituary is the $27 million he raised to launch the Cary Leeds Center for Tennis and Learning in the South Bronx in 2015. Dedicated to athletic instruction and academic enrichment for underprivileged youth—health and head—the center is also an expression of the most important thing in Leeds’s life: his family. It is named after his son, who competed in six US Opens and five Wimbledon championships before dying at the age of 45.

Sitting there in Patroon, I heard stories of how these men had committed to keeping themselves healthy and how their curiosity led them to continue to engage with the world at a point in their lives when they could just as easily have settled into insular routine. But it was the lunch itself that testified to their commitment to their hearts. Ninety years in, they were still making a point of getting together, speaking, laughing, and growing closer to other people.

Upon arriving, as we sat and introduced ourselves, Patricof explained that he wanted to do this lunch not only to learn from one another at this stage of life but also to simply enjoy socializing with peers—like many women do on a regular basis. Women live five to six more years than men; maybe it is their commitment to deep relationships, he suggested, that is one of the key factors of their greater longevity.

Women came up in another way too: Everyone attributed their health and longevity to having a loving partner.

The majority of the men have had incredibly long marriages. Nine of the 12 had been married for more than 60 years. Two had been married for 70 years. Then there was Freund, who joked that he, too, has been married for 60 years—just not all to the same woman. (He is married to his second wife, and they are at 41 years and counting.)

They all also have children and grandchildren, whom they are in touch with more than a few times a week—something any of us who are parents can do.

Rose has a unique ritual. “We have a family video conference every Sunday night with family from all parts of the country. It’s life enhancing and a time to connect and give everyone a chance to share accomplishments and disappointments with each other,” he explained.

At this stage of their lives, many have lost longtime friends, but they all strive to make new friends, some young, some old. In fact, there’s already a plan in place to replicate the lunch so that a bunch of other 90-somethings can be invited into the conversation. Here’s the thing I noticed about this: What got these guys excited about another lunch wasn’t simply that they enjoyed each other’s company or having a laugh with peers about Viagra. They were also inspired by one other. That must count for something.

I walked away inspired, too, and in awe of how these men live. They have to walk a line that most of us don’t yet, and maybe never will. Part of being alive—especially for this group—is thinking about the future, trying to figure out what’s next, planning another pursuit, but now they all admit that their horizon of looking out, say, 25 years is no longer a reality.

In the face of that tension, they’ve found a way to be optimistic about their future while at the same time savoring the present. None complained about getting older. None talked about their aches and pains. Yes, they faced growing limitations, but they all seemed to take them in stride.

“I do my best to love my life and try to have the same intensity and diligence that I had in my younger years,” said Leeds.

Langone said, “When I can no longer take care of myself and I’m totally dependent on someone else, then that’s the time to go,” adding that “God has his plan.”

And Patricof has his. He’s already publicly stated that he plans to live to 114, which would be 22 more years. I pointed out that the world’s oldest verified living person, Ethel Caterham in the UK, just turned 117, and I challenged Alan to break that record. He gladly told me that he accepts the challenge, and I don’t doubt he’ll make a real run at it. I have to admit I have an ulterior motive: I look forward to my lunch with centenarians. And supercentenarians.

#6993. Frog Closures Are the Unexpected Detail Defining Fall Jackets

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

It’s all in the details, from intricate knots to elaborate braiding

There are plenty of ways to make a statement with your outerwear, from an oversize silhouette to a high collar or bold color. But this fall, designers are turning their attention to a smaller detail: the fastening. Jackets featuring decorative knots, elaborate loops, and ornate toggles have emerged as one of the season’s biggest outerwear trends, offering an alternative to standard buttons and zippers.

In particular, frog fastenings are having a resurgence, bringing a fanciful finishing touch to everything from minimalist wool peacoats to tailored military-inspired jackets. Typically constructed from fabric or braided cord, the fasteners consist of a knotted button and a loop. Originally designed to secure garments, the closures have evolved into a more ornamental role with intricate shapes and floral-like designs.

While they may seem new for the season, frog-fastener jackets have a history that spans centuries. In Chinese dress, knotted closures known as pankou have long appeared on garments such as the qipao and tangzhuang. By the 18th and 19th centuries, frogging had also become a feature of European military uniforms, worn by cavalry regiments whose fitted jackets were adorned with braided cords.

Fashion has revisited the detail since then. Oscar de la Renta incorporated frog fastenings into his designs in the 1990s, while Valentino explored versions for its fall 2015 couture collection. More recently, for fall 2026, designers reinterpreted knotted closures: At Kallmeyer, black toggles punctuated a gilded jacquard jacket, while Kim Shui incorporated the detail into fur-trimmed blazers and opera jackets. Meanwhile, labels like Khaite and Ann Demeulemeester embraced military-inspired looks featuring ornamental braiding and embellished fronts.

Today’s store-rack versions range from collarless blazers with understated knots to dramatic outerwear designs featuring detailed cording. Brands like Róhe have introduced contemporary takes on frogging, while Hong Kong–based fashion label Sau Lee has reimagined traditional Tang-style jackets with modern silhouettes.

Rather than reinventing jackets entirely, designers are proving that something as minuscule as a fastening can make a familiar silhouette feel new again for the season. Ahead, shop our favorite takes on the look.

#6992. Please Enjoy Jenna Johnson’s Perfect Clapback to Critics and Broadway Rumors After Her ‘DWTS’ Elimination

Harry Shum Jr. and Jenna Johnson may have been eliminated ahead of the Dancing With the Stars finale, but the pro dancer isn’t quite finished yet.

After they bid farewell to the ballroom on Tuesday night, Jenna posted a hilarious clapback to critics and, in the process, seemingly debunked all those Broadway rumors. In a video shared to her Instagram account on Wednesday, November 18, Jenna and the rest of the eliminated DWTS pros performed dramatized choreography, poking fun at folks who claim she kept “putting the emphasis on herself” throughout the season.

Her song choice for the post, “I Can’t Make You Love Me” by Bonnie Raitt, was pointed and aspirationally petty, too. Some of the lyrics? Ahem: “Cause I can’t make you love me if you don’t / You can’t make your heart feel something it won’t.”

As the other dancers lifted her and danced circles around her, Jenna also referenced Harry’s polarizing “scream” during their week two Viennese waltz to “Iris” by the Goo Goo Dolls…which the internet made fun of at the time.

Harry himself even chimed in with a comment, writing, “Scream. Solo after solo. It’s like I was never there… 😂😂😂.”

Another pro, Britt Stewart, added, “It’s the scream for me.”

ICYMI, one of The Sun’s sources previously alleged Jenna had been “gunning for a Broadway role, 100 percent” when she performed a mini solo at the end of her and Harry’s performance of “Supercalifragilisticexpialidocious” earlier in the season.

“She has become more and more focused on her getting a Broadway offer after this season and after the DWTS tour ends than she is highlighting Harry and it shows,” a source told the outlet. “She put a solo of herself into the routine for Disney Night—which is basically unheard of for a pro to do. Jenna is doing a disservice to [Harry] by putting the emphasis on herself, and the other pros know it.”

Welp…seems like Jenna got the last laugh. 10/10 comeback!

#6991. Brace Yourselves, Another Harlan Coben Netflix Drama Is on the Way

The creators of I Will Find You are working on a new series.

You will not be surprised to hear that Netflix’s favorite crime-thriller maestro has had yet another commission. Myron Bolitar hasn’t even finished filming, but news has arrived that another Harlan Coben joint has been green-lit.

Netflix has just announced that it’s working on a new adaptation of Coben’s The Innocent, led by the same creative team behind I Will Find You. The novel was previously adapted as a Spanish-language series, also for Netflix, but this adaptation promises a fresh, dynamic spin on the hit story. Read on for everything we know so far about The Innocent.

The story revolves around a man named Matt Hunter, who tries to live a quiet life with his wife, Olivia, in New Jersey while concealing secrets from his mysterious past. When he and Olivia try to buy a house in his hometown, those secrets start to spill out and, worse, people start turning up dead.

Soon enough, Matt becomes the prime suspect in the murders, leaving him and Olivia to do everything they can to prove his innocence, clear his name, and protect their future together.

As the show has only just been announced, we don’t have any details of the cast yet. But Cole Galvin, Netflix’s vice president of drama series, said, “Harlan Coben is one of the great creative partnerships at Netflix, and The Innocent marks our 15th story together. Nobody builds a can’t-look-away thriller like Harlan, and Robert Hull, Bryan Wynbrandt and Steven Lilied brought their own dynamic creativity to I Will Find You, a global hit for us. We’re thrilled to welcome them to the Netflix family, and we can’t wait for the world to get hooked on their Innocent.”

There’s no information about the show’s release date, and we imagine there won’t be for a while, as there are still two other Coben novel adaptations in production at the streamer.

#6986. John Madden Deserved Better Than a Messy Amazon Biopic

A movie can have all the makings of a winner and still fumble the ball.

As a movie critic, I’m not in the business of telling professional filmmakers what they could or should do with their art. What an unproductive way to do my job. But sometimes I see something so egregious, and so boneheaded, I’m compelled to scream from my seat, forehead running hot and veins bulging out—like a coach forced to watch his wide receiver telegraph his route to the defense like a GPS in cleats. That’s how I felt watching MADDEN.

Streaming now on Prime Video, right on time for Thanksgiving football, is David O. Russell’s MADDEN. It’s a big streaming snooze where Nicolas Cage dons a fat suit and putzes around in a confused performance that flip-flops on which part of the legendary coach-turned-broadcaster-turned-foot-spray-spokesman’s interiority we’re meant to sympathize with. The real John Madden suffered cognitive decline until his death in 2021, but Cage’s performance is another exhausting exercise of actors playing undiagnosed dementia—and MADDEN has no calcium in its bones to even commit to that.

Devoid of suspense, stakes, or really any answer to the question “Why?” MADDEN is a plotless dud that falls short in its game plan of probing the unbearable weight of a massive legacy. Worst of all, the spine of a fantastic movie is all there. It’s just not in playing shape. To wit: Scrubbed from the trailers is the reason anyone under the age of 35 knows John Madden’s name: video games. (It is my personal conspiracy theory this was done to mitigate losing an audience of boomer granddads, who will fire up the movie on their Roku before passing out on tryptophan.) In 1988, the former Raiders coach who scribbled on screens lent his name to a project by a small tech company called Electronic Arts. Today it’s valued at $55 billion and owned by Saudis. But back then it was just a start-up full of nerds making a football-simulation video game called Madden NFL, which has since morphed into an industry juggernaut that rakes in the equivalent of a small country’s gross domestic product.

The men and women of Madden’s era are obviously unable to fathom computer games as a big deal. (Even now, on the eve of Grand Theft Auto VI, I have heard from people grossly underestimating its economic and cultural power.) In the film is John Mulaney as Trip Hawkins, the founder of EA, whose wiry physique, skinny ties, and plaid button-downs stand in contrast to the movie’s many beefcake extras who exude ’70s-flavored machismo. But Trip’s presence in the film exists to warn the chain-smoking dinosaurs of their coming extinction. Cementing a legacy is not about winning so much that they make a bronze bust and stick you in the Hall of Fame, with the other dead guys. It’s about making your name mean something. No one sees the future until it’s in front of them—you can only hope you can find your way to be part of it somehow. And somehow John Madden did just that. Unfortunately, even MADDEN fails to recognize the awesome potential of its own story.

Russell’s latest work spends the first half cosplaying as a sober sports biopic, in the vein of Moneyball, until it eventually and perhaps inevitably pivots into a low-stakes tech-business success story like BlackBerry or AIR. This alone isn’t a bad thing—frankly, it should be the entire movie—but MADDEN’s failure to synthesize its core themes about how men can actually live forever is what makes the movie such a frustrating experience to sit through.

MADDEN (the movie, all caps) is concerned with legacy. What does it mean? What does it really take to be remembered? For Cage’s Madden, it starts out as a straightforward idea: win Super Bowls. Football runs in his blood and runs his house; early on, Kathryn Hahn, as Madden’s wife, Virginia, scolds her son for rooting for the wrong team. Raiders football is what feeds and clothes them, she says. Thanking Raiders football is akin to thanking God. When the Raiders start winning and a retired Madden watches quite pathetically with a microphone in his hand, you can see the thousand-yard stare in his eyes as he considers abandoning his faith. Later, Christian Bale’s gel-slicked Al Davis, the owner of the Raiders whose rings form a set of brass knuckles, tries to convince Madden to come back—because winning is all it takes to be remembered, and winning is all that matters.

Before this movie, I had never heard of Al Davis, but I have played plenty of Madden on PlayStation. By the end of the movie (minor spoilers), Cage spends Thanksgiving with his preteen granddaughter, who whomps his butt with a controller in her hands. You can see what MADDEN is getting at here. Real legacy cannot be measured in rings or bronze statues but in the joy you inspire in others. Like it or not, a best-selling video game is how today’s generation of professional athletes stayed interested in the sport, and it might be John Madden’s single greatest contribution to football, more than anything he’s ever done on the field.

Unfortunately, Russell’s slipshod direction ensures that nothing in the movie clicks and nothing in it breathes. Trite dialogue and a flat visual palette that’s barely a notch above Hallmark movies keep it on the bench. As it stands, MADDEN is simply not the movie I would have made. But I’m just screaming from the nosebleeds.

#6984. I Just Finished My Kitchen Renovation, and This “Hidden” Island Feature Is the One Upgrade I’d Recommend to Everyone

It’s made my cook space so much more functional.

#6979. Movado’s New Tempo-Matic Watch Is Inspired by an Iconic Piece from the ’70s

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Proof once again that it’s hip to be square.

Movado was founded in 1881 in La Chaux-de-Fonds, Switzerland, which isn’t a particularly odd backstory for a heritage Swiss watch brand. But it has been American owned since 1983 and has a distinct quality tied deeply to its long history in the U.S. market—which puts it slightly outside the predictable purview of many watch collectors.

Right now, a growing band of horological nerds is finding rich pickings in the Movado back catalog and pushing up vintage prices considerably—especially for its mid-century chronographs and its legendary Museum Watch (which celebrates 80 years in 2027; more of that to come) in the process.

It’s not only the collectors who are digging into Movado’s past, however. Last month, the brand released the Heritage Tempo-Matic ($995–$1,095), a re-creation of a watch that merges sporty and retro design in a neat and slinky package. The Tempo-Matic, Movado’s first automatic movement, was launched in 1945 and went on to power a broad range of Movado’s wristwatches. But the rounded yet boxy “TV” shape of the new Heritage Tempo-Matic is unmistakably 1970s, when watch design took a giant leap forward into sexy new territory.

The 2026 version is available in three executions, each powered by the reliable Sellita SW200-1B movement and fitted with integrated steel bracelets that positively scream seventies. Two of them come in brushed steel with vertically brushed burgundy or blue dials. A third—which, we must admit, is our favorite—is gold-tone PVD with a warm gray dial. You’d be hard-pressed to find such an elegant, authentic, and affordable homage to the 1970s—a decade that’s currently a very hot motif in watch-collecting circles—anywhere else.

For further Movado inspiration, you might enjoy a glimpse into the brand’s rich watchmaking history at the Horological Society of New York, a fascinating nonprofit organization—effectively a guild created to support immigrant German watchmakers—that since 1866 has promoted the art and science of watchmaking in America. In the guild’s expansive watchmaking library, until July 2027, a dedicated display charts the Movado brand’s achievements in watchmaking, advertising, and design from as early as the ahead-of-its-time, art-deco-style Polyplan of 1912. Visits to the Movado exhibit are free, but we advise you to call ahead to check availability. And if you really get the bug while you’re there, you might like to sign up for membership, regular lectures, and even a series of watchmaking workshops where you get hands-on watchmaking experience under expert tutorship.

It comes in three storage sizes—128, 256, and 512GB—with the best discount on the 128GB model.

All in all, the iPad deals available right now aren’t worth writing home about—but don’t despair! These deals can literally change in an instant, and I’m predicting that prices will become comparable to last year’s Black Friday sale if you’re willing to wait until next week. #6972. The Wait Is Over: Black Friday’s Best Apple Watch Deals Are Finally Here

Just as I predicted, the Apple Watch SE 3 and Series 12 are at record-low prices.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

They say good things come to those who wait—and when it comes to Apple Watch deals, that couldn’t be more true. Amazon’s 12-day Black Friday sale is here, and just as I predicted, the Series 12 and SE 3 are both on sale for the lowest prices we’ve ever seen. However, steals I saw on the Ultra 3 and Ultra 4 earlier this month have sold out—but I’m expecting a restock.

So, should you shop these record-low deals now or wait until Black Friday (November 27)? As a tech-obsessed editor who tracks these prices all year long, I’m sharing everything you need to know below.

The best Apple Watch deal I’m seeing for Black Friday? The Apple Watch SE 3, which just returned to a record-low price of $199. It’s the most budget-friendly smartwatch in Apple’s lineup, but the S10 chip-powered device doesn’t skimp on premium features. There are tons of handy health insights to help you reach your fitness goals, from workout monitors that’ll count those 10,000 steps to sleep scores and even period cycle tracking.

This watch is available in 40mm or 44mm sizes, making it a great option if you prefer a sleeker look that won’t compete with your outfits. It also boasts up to 18 hours of battery life, with a fast-charging mode that’ll get you from 0 percent to 80 percent battery in just 45 minutes. Basically, this is your best option for a no-frills, entry-level smartwatch that won’t break the bank.

If you’re looking for more in-depth fitness insights and faster processing, the brand-new Apple Watch Series 12 is also worth adding to cart while it’s on sale for its lowest price ever. This S11 chip-powered model boasts a longer battery life than the SE 3 at up to 24 hours, and a slightly larger screen size at 42mm or 46mm. It boasts the same safety features and fitness trackers as the SE 3, plus upgraded insights like a Blood Oxygen app and hypertension notifications.

The Apple Watch Ultra 4 is the largest and most premium model in Apple’s lineup, with a 49mm case size and S11 chip. Most impressive of all, it has the longest battery life of any Apple Watch—50 hours, to be exact—which is more than double the life of the SE 3 or Series 12. It’s currently out of stock, but it was on sale for a record-low price of $700 earlier this month.

Similarly, the Apple Watch Ultra 3 (2025) is currently sold out, but it was on sale for $549 a few weeks ago. This S10 chip-powered model offers health insights like heart rhythm reports, blood oxygen scoring, and sleep score tracking, as well as safety features like crash detection and emergency SOS. Plus, it boasts an impressive 42-hour battery life to boot.

My two cents? The Apple Watch SE 3 and Series 12 are the best models for most shoppers, and they’re finally at record-low prices. I recommend scooping them up now before they inevitably sell out—no need to wait until next week. However, if you prefer a high-tech model like the Ultra 3 or 4, I predict at least one will be back in stock soon.

She specializes in fragrance, beauty, fashion, and lifestyle content for Cosmopolitan, ELLE, Good Housekeeping, Esquire, and more.
