#6908. Take Note: This Is How Cool People Are Decorating for Thanksgiving This Year

These tastemakers are bringing their personal style to the holiday.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Pumpkin centerpieces, rustic garlands, dried-fruit wreaths, and cornucopias are classic Thanksgiving decorations. They’re timeless and beloved, but they can also feel predictable. Traditional decor has its place, of course, but Thanksgiving tablescapes—and dinner parties in general—are evolving, much like interiors, to feel more personal, lived-in, and layered. So we asked nine tastemakers and experts how they’re decorating for this family-centric holiday.

From traditional touches to wholly unexpected looks, read on to see how they’re making their homes feel festive this autumn, and maybe you’ll be inspired to try something new this year.

Ever since designer Mikel Welch moved to sunny California, his family has never hesitated to go to his for Thanksgiving to escape the chilly Michigan weather. It’s become a tradition to gather at his house, and similarly to how he grew up, Welch likes to keep his decorations for the holiday understated and warm.

“I love getting the exterior of my house into full fall mode with mums, pumpkins, and seasonal greenery,” Welch tells House Beautiful. “The beauty of fall decorating is that it can carry you from October straight through Thanksgiving without having to change much.”

The designer’s house is filled with dark, moody colors and earthy textures year-round, so he focuses mainly on bringing natural elements inside to do the work for him. “I also have a little tradition with my dad,” he adds. “For the past couple of years, we've made trips to the nursery together to pick out flowers, greenery, and sometimes bales of hay for the front of the house. It's become something I really enjoy doing with him.”

“Thanksgiving has always been a big family affair,” real estate expert and maximalism extraordinaire Harvin Greene says. She’s happy to keep that tradition going as an adult, just in her own way. In the past, Greene’s aunt and uncle would have everyone at their house for the big meal, but last year, they passed the hosting baton to Greene for the first time. “I may have gotten a little carried away...in the best possible way.”

Getting to decorate two tables—one for the “adults,” another for the “kids,” even though they’re technically all grown-ups, too—Greene used this opportunity to design two completely different tablescapes. “I grew up surrounded by women who understood that a beautifully set table and a genuinely good time should go hand in hand,” she adds.

The one in the formal dining space (i.e., the “adults’” table) was theatrical and dramatic, and nearly everything on it had some sort of familial connection, from her parents’ engraved goblets to her husband’s grandmother’s sterling flatware. The kids’ table, on the other hand, was whimsical and vibrant, complete with a yellow patterned tablecloth, colorful glassware, and playful animal candlesticks. “I don't feel the need to follow the traditional Thanksgiving color palette,” Greene says. “Who decided Thanksgiving had to be orange and brown? Yellow, pink, turquoise, and green can be just as festive.”

The holiday isn’t over until they bring out the Bingo cards, a spirited Thanksgiving tradition that Greene’s Aunt Jean started—which involves cash prizes. “It's one of those family traditions that makes the holiday feel like ours, no matter whose house we're celebrating in.”

Talia Hubble is no stranger to a good dinner party—much of her content actually centers around her love of hosting. But Thanksgiving is a bit extra special for the Rhode Islander, especially since it’s being held at her cottage this year. “Growing up, it was just me and my mom, and she was too busy to decorate,” Hubble says. “I think that’s part of why I’ve fallen in love with seasonal decorating as an adult, I get to bring all my cozy childhood decorating dreams to life!”

As soon as the calendar switches from October to November, Hubble starts decorating for fall, opting for an eclectic mix of subtle Christmas decor (think bows, garlands, and wreaths), an excessive amount of taper candles, and a traditional Thanksgiving palette of oranges, browns, greens, and reds. Every year looks a little bit different, but there are pieces in her collection that are simply too cute not to bring out every holiday season.

“Half the fun of pulling everything out for Thanksgiving is remembering where it came from,” she says. “I think the best decor is a culmination of pieces you’ve been collecting for years, and the memories behind those pieces are what make the table feel special and uniquely yours.”

By the time she’s done decorating, her home feels like a warm, welcoming escape, somewhere her guests can exhale. “My philosophy is that you can’t go wrong when you aim for cozy, colorful, and embrace a little organized mess,” Hubble adds. “My favorite Thanksgiving tables are the ones that look even better halfway through dinner, when the napkins are rumpled and everyone’s reaching for seconds.”

Chef and food stylist Romilly Newman can’t always count on being in the same place as her family for Thanksgiving since everyone’s scattered about, but she always makes a point to throw an autumnal dinner with friends around the holiday time, complete with beautiful fall decor.

Her mom never decorated their home while she was growing up with specific Thanksgiving decorations—instead, she would decorate with deep orange and red flowers to capture the fall vibe. Newman’s style as an adult is similar. “I am fall-obsessed, so as soon as the temperature drops from summer, I'm setting up branches, candles, and darker accents,” she says. “I love the atmosphere inside and outside around Thanksgiving time, and I try my best to bottle that up in my home.”

Natural thicker linen, pops of silver, dark florals, and moody lighting envelop her space as the chef relies mostly on candlelight and organic elements—like figs, sage, chestnuts, and mandarins—as the decorative accents.

“Thanksgiving is great because it brings family together and offers time to enjoy food, friends, and being at home,” Newman adds. “Whatever your style is, try to create an environment that allows for great conversation and a festive evening. You want everyone to be comfortable and feel the holiday spirit.”

When writer Olivia Noceda was a child, she would stay up late the night before Thanksgiving and design custom menus for everyone on Microsoft Paint, which she would then print out, frame, and place on each table setting in preparation for the big meal. The holiday was a big deal for Noceda’s mother, who would always put on the most beautiful Thanksgiving gatherings. “I think that's where my love for hosting and setting a beautiful table really started,” she explains.

However, for the last six years, Noceda has been having Thanksgiving with her husband’s side of the family, so when she does host around the holiday, it’s typically for Friendsgiving. “I don't necessarily pick a theme, but I do like everything to feel cohesive,” she explains of her decorating process. “I usually start incorporating little autumnal touches sometime in October and let them carry me through Thanksgiving.”

The writer and recipe developer lets the season guide her, building her tablescape and decor around a beautiful branch from the farmers’ market, a bowl of pears or persimmons, a collection of little pumpkins and squash, or a few taper candles on the dining room table. Ingredients play a big part in her decor—she can even take a fresh loaf of bread and make it feel special on her table.

For the rest of her decor, she likes to incorporate vintage serving pieces, everyday elements, and elevated details like antique silver and linen napkins with things she already owns to lean into that lived-in, warm feeling. The last thing she wants is her home to feel like a department store display. “Nothing needs to match perfectly, and I actually prefer when it doesn't,” Noceda adds.

What entrepreneur and Real Housewives of New York star Erin Lichy remembers best about Thanksgivings growing up is the smell. “We always spent Thanksgiving out east, and the house was full of pumpkins, local hay, and lanterns,” she explains. The scent of that fresh produce, mixed with her grandmother’s homemade turkey t’bit, has shaped the way she decorates for and hosts Thanksgiving. “That coveted and highly personal (and incredibly delicious) recipe is in my cookbook, She’s a Host, and it’s part of every Thanksgiving I host.”

Decorating starts in the kitchen for her family. Lichy wants the home to smell like fall, so she focuses on that sense, making seasonal recipes with squash and pumpkin and incorporating autumnal flowers in her interiors. The melding of those scents gives her townhouse a warm feeling, especially when mixed with layered fall colors and the crackling of the fireplace.

Ever since Lichy and her family have moved into their townhouse, she’s been obsessed with decorating her stoop too. “The stoop is the first thing everyone sees, so this year I decided to bring in Simon Wallace Design to make it really stand out!” Lichy adds. “They completely transformed it with layers of seasonal color and texture, and it feels so warm and welcoming.” She specifically loves using the faux garlands and pinecones, as well as lanterns, from Balsam Hill and Frontgate because they’re so beautifully made that they look real, and she’s able to reuse them year after year.

No matter what she does to decorate though, she just wants her home to feel warm and lived-in, and this is the season and holiday to really lean into that.

Decorating for the holidays is Gabby Amato’s version of a self-care day. “It's truly one of my favorite days of the year,” she says. “I carve out a weekend afternoon when my husband and son are out of the house, light my favorite fall candles, turn on some music, and have the house entirely to myself.”

Amato, the founder of Table Two Studio, a paper plate brand which specializes in beautiful, artistic designs that you definitely won’t want to throw away, credits her love of Thanksgiving and the fall season in general to her mother. While growing up in the ‘90s, she saw her mom put so much care and effort into the holiday decorations, always making their home feel warm, nostalgic, and full of spirit. “For Thanksgiving, she decorated our mantel with gourds, greenery, and ceramic pilgrim statues that were a gift from my grandmother,” Amato says. “We had oversized cornstalks and pumpkins at the front door. Nothing ever felt overly styled or precious.”

It’s this feeling that has stuck with her throughout her life, and it’s what she hopes she captures in her own home every year. She begins decorating for the fall early, right after Labor Day, pulling out old pieces and unboxing anything new and arranging it all in fresh ways so nothing feels stale. “I love beautiful decor, of course, but I'm much more interested in creating an atmosphere than achieving a particular aesthetic,” she adds. “I want my home to feel warm, lived-in, a little nostalgic, and festive.”

Donna Lennard’s family was never one for holiday dressing—most of the attention at Thanksgiving fell on the delicious food shared with family. As a restauranteur and cofounder of beloved NYC establishment il Buco, it makes sense why the meal remains to be the most important thing to Lennard even today.

“Clearly, the table is always festive, whether it’s the tablecloth, the napkins, or linens that reflect the season in burnt orange and the like,” she explains. “I love to decorate a table with assorted colorful gourds and seasonal florals, beautiful candlelight (our beeswax candles already have an orange glow), orange ranunculus, and the occasional dried floral.” Colorful Pienza glassware dots the table and her plethora of beautiful tableware gives it more character, but she keeps everything else relatively simple to ensure the food remains the center of attention.

Though Lennard doesn’t often host her family and friends for the Thanksgiving meal, she’s already looking forward to next year when everyone will finally gather at her beach house.

Being from Australia, Kendall Knox—the cofounder of Olive Ateliers along with her husband—didn’t have a personal reference of what the holiday “should” be or look like. This gives her the freedom of deciding what it is for her family. “What it has become is an intimate table: my husband Ben, our son Rhodes, and Ben's mum,” she says. “No rotation, no kids' table, no forty-pound bird. Just the four of us and a dinner that runs long because nobody is in a hurry to leave it.”

Since Knox isn’t beholden to the classic Thanksgiving decor, she tends to keep her home and her table rather simple, “shopping the house,” as she puts it to decorate instead of buying something new. She keeps a rather neutral palette of rich browns, creamy whites, terracotta, and silver, then uses antique linens, mismatched silverware, and candlesticks that have collected wax to capture the true lived-in feel.

Her focus tends to be on the table and the meal, letting the food add more color. If a tablecloth has a wine ring on it from the year before, all the better—it complements the stacked plates at the end of the meal.

“The objects that come out every year, that get a little more marked each time, are the ones that end up meaning the most,” Knox adds. “Thanksgiving is just a very good excuse to use them.”

On top of being the magazine's go-to Waco expert, Meghan also touches on quite a few other verticals in the digital space, like hot news content featuring your favorite HGTV stars, home design inspiration, and the latest interior TikTok trends. When she's not interviewing interior designers about home trends, you can find Meghan reading a fictional thriller with her cat in her lap, planning themed parties, or strolling through flea markets searching for chunky candle holders and vintage bookends.

#6907. Mckenna Grace Is Winning the Method Dressing Hunger Games

We spoke to her stylist, Molly Dickson, about creating Mckenna's killer carpet looks for the Sunrise on the Reaping press tour.

Method dressing for press tours is part of the reason 2026’s year of movies feels so unstoppable. The carpets for the latest Hunger Games film, Sunrise on the Reaping, have been heating up look after look, but no one is catching fire quite like Mckenna Grace. Along with her stylist, Molly Dickson, the pair have been turning out absolute masterpieces, with extraordinary commitment to Hunger Games literary lore: As small as ladybugs in Mckenna’s hair (a nod to the ‘Ladybug, Ladybug’ song Haymitch Abernathy sings to Maysilee Donner in Sunrise on the Reaping) to step out in NYC, and as large as a recreation of Katniss’s truly iconic wedding dress from The Hunger Games 2: Catching Fire.

“Mckenna was absolutely certain from the beginning that she wanted to embrace method dressing for this press tour, and I was completely on board,” Molly tells Cosmopolitan.

The concept of method dressing isn’t new, but it’s been incredibly impactful on movie press runs in particular over the past few years. In practice, actors wear looks that reference niche details of the films they’re promoting. Think Zendaya’s Greek-goddess-forward looks for The Odyssey, Margot Robbie’s Barbie ensembles pulled straight from the dolls themselves, Ariana Grande and Cynthia Erivo in a lifetime’s worth of pink and green for Wicked.

“I think the best method dressing moments are the ones that tell a story, while still feeling like incredible fashion looks of their own,” says Molly. “We wanted the fans to have those moments of recognition and excitement, but we also wanted someone who knew nothing about The Hunger Games to look at Mckenna and simply think she looked incredible!”

So how did they do it? What references were on their mood board? What dress almost never saw the light of day? And most importantly: What’s the story behind THE legendary arena dress from the UK premiere? Superstar stylist Molly Dickson answers our most pressing press tour related questions.

It was a really collaborative process between the two of us. Mckenna would come to me with ideas, and I would figure out how to translate them into fashion. She was the one who said, ‘Ladybugs are part of the movie, we need to do a ladybug look!’ From there, I started sourcing pieces that either incorporated ladybugs directly or finding more unexpected, interesting ways to reference them.

We were very intentional about making sure every look had a connection to some part of the Hunger Games universe. We wanted the references to feel thoughtful and recognizable to the fans, while still creating beautiful fashion moments that felt authentic to Mckenna and her personal style.

I don't think either of us initially realized just how invested the Hunger Games fans would be in all the little references and Easter eggs! Once we started seeing that reaction, it became more fun to dig deeper into the franchise and pull inspiration from different characters and iconic moments. Mckenna knows this world so well, and I loved being able to bring her ideas to life.

Mckenna really wanted to pay homage to the strong female characters throughout the franchise—specifically Katniss and Joanna Mason—which became the inspiration for so many of our looks. As we started fittings, we found ways to incorporate inspiration from Rue as well.

We wanted a mix of more recognizable references and subtle nods rather than exact recreations of costumes from the movies. We used the original costumes and characters as our starting point, but then put our own fashion interpretation on them. I think that's where my creativity really comes in. You want fans to recognize the inspiration, but you also want the looks to feel fresh, elevated, and true to the person wearing them.

I really can't think of a better way to generate excitement and conversation around a movie through fashion than method dressing. It's such a fun way to bring the story beyond the screen, and give fans something deeper to connect with. What I loved most about this tour was seeing the conversations happening online, not just about the fashion itself, but about the characters, the references, and ultimately the film.

I think my personal favorite was Mckenna’s Paris look inspired by Katniss's wedding gown. It was such a major fashion moment—we actually went back and forth on whether it might be too much! Mckenna’s team really encouraged us to go for it, and they were absolutely right.

The most challenging part was figuring out how to recreate the sculptural silver elements. Michael Ngo made experimental custom silver pieces for us, which we paired with a beautiful vintage Monique Lhuillier wedding gown. I loved that the complete look felt like a true tribute to Katniss's iconic gown, but still had its own fashion identity. It was one of those moments where the creativity, collaboration, and a little bit of risk-taking really paid off!

We really wanted to pay homage to the strong female characters throughout the Hunger Games franchise while still staying true to Mckenna's personal style. We wanted each look to tell a story and have a connection to the films, but never feel like she was wearing a costume.

For the press junkets and more casual appearances, we focused on subtle nods to the movies, whether that was through color, texture, or small details that fans might recognize. For the bigger red carpet moments, we had the opportunity to be a little more dramatic and lean into those bigger, more established references. The fun of this tour was finding that balance between fashion and storytelling!

Honestly, I have to give all the credit to Mckenna’s mom for this one! She actually discovered the dress on Instagram and sent it over to me. The brand is ALmée, and they’re based in Vietnam.

I love that this look came together in such an unexpected way. It wasn't something we had custom-designed or planned months in advance. It was really just Mckenna's mom spotting something incredible and all of us realizing how perfectly it fit into the Hunger Games world.

That's one of my favorite things about styling: Sometimes the most exciting looks come from the most unexpected places, and this was definitely one of those moments!

One of the looks I was most excited about, but also the most stumped on creatively, Katniss's iconic Girl on Fire gown. The challenge was figuring out how to translate that moment into something that felt like a beautiful red carpet gown rather than a costume.

Cong Tri immediately came to mind because of their intricate beadwork and craftsmanship. I knew I wanted the fire gown to incorporate beading because of the way it catches the light, especially under red carpet lighting and camera flashes. I felt that was going to be key to creating the illusion of flames and bringing the dress to life.

It was honestly the perfect collaboration. The Cong Tri team was so willing to push the design creatively. After experimenting with different fabrics, textures, and techniques, the end result was exactly what we had hoped for. The dress felt dramatic and powerful, with that obvious nod to Katniss, but still felt like a really beautiful fashion moment. That's always the goal with these kinds of looks!

Ali Royals loves true stories and hates writing in the third person, even though she’s doing it right now. Ali is a poet, writer, and denier of any Carrie Bradshaw allegations. Follow her on Instagram and Substack for her daily Outfit Prompts, where she tells herself stories in order to get dressed.

#6903. Jack O’Connell on McQueen, Ink, and the “Heavy Hitters” of Sinners

He may not be James Bond, but the actor is still dressed to kill.

Jack O’Connell may not be James Bond, but he’s still dressed to kill. At the 17th annual Governors Awards, hosted by the Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences, the Sinners and 28 Years Later: The Bone Temple star rolled up in a mono-black suit sourced from McQueen’s Spring/Summer 2027 pre-collection: a tailored jacket with ultrawide lapels, black wool barathea trousers, and polished Jermyn brogues. If anyone at the awards exuded main-character energy, it was O’Connell.

“It’s a real lovely, beautiful chance to congregate and celebrate,” O’Connell tells me days later. “You come away from them feeling really inspired. There’s a lot of love in that room.”

He adds, “It is my mission now to binge Glenn Close [movies].” (The 79-year-old legend received an Academy Honorary Award for her many decades of contributions to the industry.)

But O’Connell’s all-black eveningwear, courtesy of McQueen, is award worthy on its own. No stranger to events like these, “sometimes you don’t wanna look uniform,” O’Connell says. The now grown-up James Cook from Skins opted for a double-breasted jacket with distinctly large lapels that he says were “almost a winged tip.” He felt it was something Alexander McQueen himself might have imagined. “Just a dapper bit of Lee McQueen—it’s nice, isn’t it? To push the boat out a little bit.”

He credits his stylist, Luke Day, for pointing him in the right direction. “We pick our moments. Sometimes we want to toe the line; sometimes we don’t. You don’t always feel like slapping a dicky bow tie on. It’s great. It mixes it up.”

When O’Connell isn’t mixing it up in front of flashing cameras, he likes to dress to his surroundings—as any man ought to. “If I’m in the countryside, it’s boots. If I’m in the city, it’s kicks. I really like a lot of Axel Arigatos on my feet. But Reebok was always the staple. I love a Reebok.”

The man could use a pair of Reeboks, because he’s had quite the year running ahead. In 2025, he was a most memorable menace in Ryan Coogler’s vampire scorcher Sinners. He kicked off 2026 in 28 Years Later: The Bone Temple as Jimmy Crystal, the bleach-blond cult leader of butt-kicking devil worshippers in the zombie apocalypse. He will soon star in Ink (which hits select theaters December 11 before it lands on Netflix on January 8), Danny Boyle’s screen adaptation of the stage play about the British tabloid The Sun. If it bleeds, it leads, indeed.

All the while, his name got thrown into the mix as the next James Bond. Though O’Connell had public support from friends like Boyle, he downplayed how much he was actually in the running (as in an October interview with Entertainment Weekly). Now that Robert Pattinson is 007, he doesn’t have to answer those questions anymore. “He’s one of the best in the biz, isn’t he? I love his work,” O’Connell says of R-Patz. “He’s a great set of hands. Anything he turns to, he just kinda nails. Really interesting actor, makes very unobvious choices. I’ve been a fan of his for a long time.”

Without Bond looming over him, O’Connell is free to focus on what he’s actually cooking up. Lately, he’s been hard at work on two big blockbusters for 2027: Godzilla x Kong: Supernova and A Quiet Place Part III. “It’s been one of my favorites for sure,” O’Connell says, reflecting on the past year. “Tied for going to the Oscars and watching my friends win trophies, it is up there as one of the best experiences of my life. I hope we keep pushing.”

So what can audiences expect to see in Supernova? Besides monster wrestling, of course. “We follow Kaitlyn [Dever]’s character up the ranks,” he explains. “She’s studying these monsters. Her brother’s part of a new organization. I can’t give too much away about it. And Trapper’s just being Trapper. You know how Trapper is.”

The project, with its green screens and CGI kaiju, is the biggest O’Connell has undertaken yet. But the large-scale endeavor brings him back to his childhood. “I remember kids running around the playground pretending to be Godzilla,” he says. “It’s omnipresent, isn’t it?”

He adds, “It’s not every job that I’m doing that type of thing. It was great to explore it to the maximum. You know who the main characters are. It’s not the people. It’s great supporting these pixelated legends.”

O’Connell can say even less about A Quiet Place Part III. “It’s in the aftermath of this horrific apocalyptic event,” he says. “It’s a tale of trauma, loss. It’s stuntier than any of us anticipated. But at the center of it is this very beautiful human story where we’re saying, What is the cost of all this fucking chaos and carnage, you know?”

But in some ways, O’Connell is still riding the high of Sinners. “People responded to it in such a way,” he says about the Oscar-winning thriller in retrospect. “It was great to see people unite at the cinema en masse. It’s great when one film can do that. I put it down to Ryan Coogler, our cast, and our entire team across the board. Everywhere you looked, it was packed with heavy hitters.”

Next time you catch O’Connell, bet he’s dressed in McQueen. “I really love what Alexander McQueen, as a person and as a creative, stood for,” he says. “We’re from similar backgrounds, which is rare for me in this world. It makes it meaningful. I celebrate his story, so any opportunity to work with them is a really heartfelt one.”

He swears you’ve seen nothing yet. “I don’t feel like I’ve done my best work yet. Far from it.”

Clothing by McQueen Spring Summer 2027 Pre-collection, wearing a sharp lapel tailored jacket and trousers in black wool barathea, a harness shirt in black cotton poplin with safety pin embroidery, and Jermyn brogues in black polished leather.

#6901. After Dating Brothers on ‘SLOMW,’ ‘The Circle’ Stars Layla Taylor and Kate Racker Consider Themselves “Sisters” (Exclusive)

Their Secret Lives of Mormon Wives lore is complicated, but their Circle strategy is simple: lean into “the douchebag.”

A new season of The Circle is here, but it’s not just civilians competing this time around. In the Hulu revival series, celebrities are also part of the competition, including The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives star Layla Taylor—though her fellow competitors don’t know that, since she’s playing as a former Mormon bisexual bro named Doug. She’s not doing it alone, either, as she entered the Circle with her bestie Kate Racker. Who is Kate, you ask? I paid a visit to her and Layla’s apartment in Atlanta to get to know her (and her SLOMW lore) a little better.

As you might recall, Kate was first introduced during SLOMW season 2 as Chase McWhorter’s on-again, off-again girlfriend. She later explained to People that she and Chase met just three weeks after she split from her ex-husband (with whom she shares a son, Rowan), and it was through Chase that she met the MomTok crew, including Layla (though fun fact: Kate already knew Jessi Draper, who was her neighbor growing up, as well as Jacob Neeley, who she used to work with in private equity).

It wasn’t until summer 2025, however, that Layla and Kate really cemented their friendship. “Before she started dating Mason [McWhorter], and I was kind of off with Chase, Layla and I were just single together. So we’d go out, we talk every day,” Kate previously told People. “We were kind of each other’s person. We’d FaceTime 24/7 and just got really, really, really close.”

Now they’re spending 24/7 together in the Circle, where—according to Kate—they’ve only gotten into “one tiny little tiff” so far. “We are sisters at this point, and I consider her my sister,” she says, adding, “I think the thing about me and Layla is we’re both like pretty sensitive, but we both love each other so much, and we’re committed [to winning]. Like, I have goosebumps.”

My big question for them: Why did they decide to step into the Circle as a catfish rather than themselves?

“It’s kind of fun to like step into somebody that you’re not,” Layla says.

Kate agrees, adding, “It’s a new experience. I mean, we’re all about new experiences and trying new things and role play. We love role play. We’ll try anything three times.”

And if you’re wondering whether they have a certain man (or a certain pair of brothers) in mind while playing “Doug,” the answer is...not really. “I feel like he’s kind of a collection of the men that we have encountered in our life or even dated in the past,” explains Layla. “Like, all of them.”

“I think we’re trying to lean into the douchebag,” adds Kate. “Not to say all men are douchebags, but no, there’s some great ones. Or, you know, a few.”

Layla then clarifies, “We’re not men haters. No, not at all. We are men observers. And we’ve observed poor behavior from the men that we do know, and we’re just emulating and dramatizing that.”

I also ask Layla if she’s missing her girlfriend Kelsie Chesser while she’s in the Circle, and she says that—while she says, “For sure, you know, I love her dearly”—the harder part is being away from her kids. “Yeah, we both cry every day,” Layla says. “Cried myself to sleep last night. But Kate is my bestie, and there’s no one I’d rather be trapped in here with.”

Since there are players who have yet to be revealed in the Circle, I need to know: Which of the Mormon Wives would they be most excited to see pop up on their screen?

“I would love to see Jessi,” Kate says. “Obviously, Miranda [Hope]. Miranda would be great. Mayci [Neeley]. Like, any of them.”

Layla grins before adding, “Yeah, I think I can say everyone...but one.”

#6898. Dreaming of Buying a Home Abroad? These 10 Countries Make It Shockingly Simple

If you’ve always wanted to live out your expat fantasies, start here.

Over the last few years, many Americans have daydreamed about life abroad. Moving internationally can often feel like a pipe dream, but with the right planning, it’s possible. However, lengthy visa processes, daunting language barriers, and a difficult-to-navigate legal system can make the dream feel more like a nightmare.

Certain countries, like Australia, Canada, and New Zealand, have bans on foreign buyers. Others, like Thailand, Cuba, and Vietnam, don’t allow outsiders to own property at all. On the other end of the spectrum are countries actively trying to attract foreign buyers, offering enviable incentives like affordable home prices, a smoother customs process, and an easier-to-navigate work-visa approval process.

Choosing the right location can make moving abroad much less intimidating. Ahead, we’re breaking down the ten best countries for Americans looking to buy property abroad. Whether you’re dreaming of relocating or searching for your perfect vacation home, these countries make buying as easy as possible and offer gorgeous landscapes too.

If you’ve ever dreamed of living among medieval architecture and rolling mountain ranges, Georgia might be the key to unlocking your fantasy. A bridge between Eastern Europe and Western Asia, the transcontinental country borders Russia, Turkey, Armenia, and the Black Sea, offering both subtropical and high-altitude climates. Thankfully, the real estate landscape is much less complex, with few legal or bureaucratic hurdles for American buyers, no residency or visa required (for up to a year), and remarkably fast processing times. In fact, the only major restriction in the Georgia market is that foreigners cannot purchase or own agricultural land, a nod to the importance of the local, small-scale farming cultures peppered across the country’s regions.

Uruguay’s buying process could not be more straightforward. South America’s second-smallest sovereign country requires no citizenship, visa, or prior approvals. Under Uruguayan law, foreign buyers are given the same rights as citizens and enjoy the benefits of free capital flow. Locals are just as, if not more, tourist-friendly. The country is known for its hospitality, laid-back café culture, and stunning coastal landscapes.

Instead of retiring to Florida, consider this balmy, beautiful Caribbean country—just a four-hour direct flight from the New York area. Like everything else on the island, the real estate laws are relatively relaxed, making buying a house easy-breezy; and you can buy and own property without obtaining residency status or special permissions, as is the case in many other Caribbean islands. “Foreigners can own property outright in the Dominican Republic, with legal protections for foreign investors,” says Mauricio Umansky, CEO and founder of The Agency. One important consideration is financing: You can certainly get a mortgage in the DR, but it may not be quite as cushy as what you can get in the U.S.—so pay cash if possible.

Great news if you’ve been wanting a more provincial life: Americans face zero restrictions when buying homes in France. The open market allows for statesiders to buy houses, apartments, or plots of land in the city, countryside, and coastal towns in person or remotely without needing citizenship, residency, or even a local visa. However, a purchase does not grant residency, so if you’re hoping to make France your permanent home, you’ll want to apply for a separate long-stay visa and note the Schengen 90-out-of-180-day rule. While buying may be simple, the finances are anything but. Closing fees, including notary fees, typically add seven to ten percent to your purchase. Mortgage rates are also incredibly hard to secure for U.S.-based buyers, thanks to strict debt-to-income caps and FATCA compliance. To simplify the process, many buyers opt to put 20 percent to 40 percent down in cash.

Perhaps the most American-friendly market, Panama boasts a large English-speaking market, virtually no restrictions on foreign property ownership, and uses the U.S. dollar as legal tender in a vastly affordable market. The Central American nation also offers residency programs like the Friendly Nations Visa, clearing the way legally for residency by investment. In addition to an efficient legal system and reliable market, Panama boasts breathtaking beaches, lush rainforests, and exceptional wildlife.

If you’re hoping for a home in the coffee valleys of Colombia, you’ll need to hire a real estate lawyer before you start scouting for mountain chalets, coffee triangle treehouses, and rainforest retreats. The Caribbean country opts out of standardized agent licensing systems, making legal aid essential to verify title and freedom certificates. While citizenship isn’t required, you will need to secure a tourist visa and Colombian Tax ID to complete your purchase and register for property taxes.

The buying process in Greece is lengthy, though it is streamlined, with a clear checklist for American buyers to find success in the market. Hiring a local lawyer or notary is a great first step after scouting properties. Not only are they familiar with title checks and contracts, but they can also navigate the intricacies that come with buying in designated border areas or near military zones, which require special clearance for non–EU buyers. While the most popular areas for American buyers, like Athens and Crete, don’t come with heavy legal intricacies, buying any property in Greece will mean securing a Greek Tax Number—a small price to pay for cities with such rich histories and breathtaking seaside views.

The people in this popular Western European coastal country are famously friendly and inviting, and that extends to real estate. According to Samira Easton, a certified international property specialist with Premier Sotheby’s International Realty, Portugal offers a Golden Visa Program, which makes it simple to buy property and gain residency; clear real estate laws; and a well-established network of English-speaking real estate agents, lawyers, and notaries to help you during the process.

Belize is home to one of the most vibrant, tourist-friendly cultures in Central America, mixing Mayan, Hispanic, Asian, and Creole cultures in its dense rainforests and rolling low plains. Buying here is as easy as it can get, as outright ownership applies to foreigners, and a British-inspired legal system abolishes language barriers and makes contracts easier to understand. Even more, there is no capital gains tax in Belize, and property taxes trend low. The country also boasts vibrant marine life and is home to the world’s second-largest barrier reef.

Spain may have ended its investor visa program in 2025, but the real estate market is still remarkably easy for Americans to navigate. The country currently imposes no restrictions on foreign or nonresident property ownership, but there are a few things you’ll need to procure before you buy, like a Foreigner Identification Number, which is mandatory for taxes. Despite the minor hurdles, the market is booming both on the ground and online. “There are also numerous online resources and property portals available, so it’s easy to find listings and information,” Easton says. This is a boon for those who aren’t able to make the trip over to Europe to house shop in person.Note that buying property does not grant you residency or bypass the country’s 90-day Schengen limit. If you’re hoping to turn your Spanish holiday into your everyday, you’ll have to apply for a separate visa.

#6896. 20 Years Later, the Best Affordable-Luxury Gift for Teen Girls Is Still a Longchamp Tote

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

There’s a lot of chatter—and, let’s face it, uncertainty—about what teen girls actually want as gifts. I track shopping trends for a living and also happen to be a mom of a Gen Z daughter, so I know how often they change their minds about what’s cool. Fortunately, my daughter is always happy to give me an honest, up-to-date report.

When she graduated from high school, we wanted to get her something nice. More importantly, we wanted to get her something she really wanted. I have to admit I was surprised when she told us she wanted Longchamp’s Le Pliage tote. That’s right, that tote. The one that was all the rage in the mid-aughts.

“It was around 2006 when my best friends and I gifted each other Longchamp totes for our birthdays,” Deputy Managing Editor Cristina Corvino tells me. “They seemed like gold to our teen selves at the time. Who knew they'd still be coveted teen gifts 20 years later? I feel validated!”

On a practical level, getting my daughter an under-$200 designer bag turned out to be a great idea. The tote is made of water-resistant nylon with a leather handle and flap, and I like that it zips all the way shut. It has a roomy main compartment, and it sits comfortably on her shoulder. To this day, it’s the tote she carries around her college campus and the one she brings with her when she comes home to visit.

But, honestly? When I bought it, I had no idea how popular this specific tote is with teen girls. Several of her friends also have the Le Pliage, and when we dropped her off at college, I couldn’t help but notice its iconic silhouette whenever yet another college kid strolled by with one on their arm.

So if you’re looking for a meaningful, decidedly grown-up gift for your stylish teen—and your wallet has grown tired of chasing one fleeting trend after another—I’m willing to bet they’d be thrilled to get a Longchamp. If they already have the Le Pliage tote (clearly, I wouldn’t be surprised), here are a few more thoughtful, under-$250 Longchamp gifts to consider.

Longchamp Le Pliage Xtra Leather Flap Card Case

Longchamp Le Pliage Energy Recycled Nylon Belt Bag

Longchamp Mini Le Pliage Xtra AirPods Case

Longchamp Longchamp Le Pliage Tea Cup Sunglasses

Drawing on more than 20 years of digital experience, Jess focuses on curating gift guides, tracking down the best deals, and testing products for candid reviews. Prior to Hearst, her career spanned Bonnier Corp., NBC Universal, and Barnes&Noble.com. #6892. The 27 Best Video Games of 2026 (So Far)

With Grand Theft Auto VI finally in gamers’ hands, 2026 might go down as one of the greatest years in the medium’s history.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Will 2026 go down as one of the best years in video games? There just simply aren’t enough hours in the calendar year to play through all of the amazing games that came out before Thursday, November 19, and now we have to add on hundreds of hours of Grand Theft Auto VI.

Of course, the industry’s economic woes cannot be overstated. It’s a dark time when even prolific studios downsize, slash budgets, and lay off talented developers with invaluable institutional knowledge. Even the GTA VI designers—whose game is already heralded as a masterpiece—are still fighting for better working conditions. So it’s hard to actually call 2026 a great year in gaming when it’s also been a year of heartbreak and uncertainty for the future.

But the games are still great. Even just a few hours into GTA VI, it’s obvious that it’s a massive achievement for Rockstar Games that’s well worth the wait. And just this past month, Nintendo dropped a remake of The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time that features beautifully upgraded graphics from the original 1998 classic. Elsewhere, Resident Evil Requiem nailed the franchise’s decades-long identity split with a game that’s half third-person action and half first-person survival horror. Plus, action games like 007 First Light, Control Resonant, and Onimusha: Way of the Sword let loose the power fantasies we’ve only dreamed of.

But it’s not all blood and guts and guns and swords. Earlier this year, the coming-of-age Mixtape caused a ruckus among gaming circles, though anyone who’s played it and loved it will tell you just how much it’s a love letter to bygone summer days. Oh, and a spin-off Pokémon title about building adorable towns and protecting the environment is currently the highest-rated game in the franchise’s thirty-year history.

So, unless you’re already dedicated to spending all of Thanksgiving and Christmas in GTA VI’s Vice City, some of the best games Esquire’s editors have played through in 2026 so far.

GTA VI hadn’t even been out for twenty-four hours yet and critics were already calling it a masterpiece. To be fair, video game critics call a lot of titles masterpieces, but it was clear from everyone’s immediate reaction to Vice City that Rockstar Games spent the last decade making something truly special. Sure, there are hundreds of hours of side quests and town-foolery to get lost in, but GTA VI also features a Bonnie & Clyde-esque love story between its two playable protagonists—Jason and Lucia—which breaks up the beloved smash-and-grab chaos of the Grand Theft Auto franchise and makes the gamer question just where the point between a video game and real life begins.

At its most basic, Nintendo’s The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake isn’t all that different from the classic, 1998 original. The game certainly looks a lot better on the Nintendo Switch 2 than it did on the Nintendo 64, now that the polygonal characters and sharp outdoors have been converted to more living and breathing graphics. But at the end of the day, you’re still playing the same game that you played in 1998. Don’t take that as a knock, though! Ocarina of Time still tops “Best Video Games of All Time” lists for a reason.

There are times playing Star Wars: Galactic Racer when you will feel like you are going too fast. And yes, the rate at which it will feel like that will shock you. But that’s pod racing! And better than any racing sim, let alone a Star Wars video game with voice acting as good as Galactic Racer, it truly feels like you’re speeding around in a hunk-a-junk that you just cobbled together with space garbage and jet engines.

Following up Fire Emblem: Three Houses was already a tall task, let alone having to follow Fire Emblem: Engage. Both titles combined the best of Japanese RPGs with the new slice-of-life school relationship activities introduced in Three Houses. And now Fortune’s Weave, something of a trilogy-capper for the series, is the most maximalist entry yet. With dozens of characters, choices, a vast map, and four unique story quests that all overlap, there’s so much to do in the latest Fire Emblem game that they could wait another five years before the next entry.

The Blood of Dawnwalker is the debut game from Rebel Wolves, a new studio started by former creatives from The Witcher 3: Blood Hunt. So if Dawnwalker feels eerily like a Witcher game, you’re not crazy. That being said, the vampire game’s controversial timer—which forces players to make quick decisions with big impacts on gameplay—fully separated the RPG and proved that the new studio was ready to try out exciting ideas all its own.

Remedy’s Alan Wake 2 topped Esquire’s Best Video Games of 2023 list three years ago, so you can bet we were excited for the studio’s next game, Control Resonant. The latest is a sequel to 2019’s Control, which now finds its former protagonist Jesse Faden missing. So, support character Dylan Faden, now in the spotlight, takes control to deal with the foreign entities and save his estranged sister.

Can video games have too much of a good thing? Apparently not. Phantom Blade Zero, the umpteenth samurai RPG since Sekiro: Shadows Die Twice and Ghost of Tsushima, dazzled gamers with its combat and fluid martial arts combos. And while it firmly isn’t a soulslike—the developer explicitly rejects the genre, actually—that still doesn’t mean Phantom Blade Zero is a walk in the park either.

How about a game that’s a little less stressful? If you need a break from the fast-paced action, check out Big Walk, a co-operative puzzle game about, well, going on a long walk. The charming game allows players to communicate only if they’re close to one another on Big Walk’s big map, providing a bit of a challenge as you progress on your journey while also adding some intimacy to the digital experience. Plus, the characters are cute as hell.

Star Wars fans ate good this year. If racing isn’t your thing, check out Zero Company, a turn-based tactics game that falls somewhere in between Fire Emblem and XCOM. Though the game isn’t all that challenging on easier difficulties, players have found a lot of fun and repeat plays with the game’s permadeath mode that doesn’t allow characters to return after they die.

Rock out with your claws out. Insomniac applies its winning formula for triple-A superhero games to flesh out the most popular X-Men member of all time. The five-foot-four berserker stars in his own solo adventure in a universe where mutants live in secret. While piecing together his own memories, Wolverine meets a telepath named Jean Grey. Together they must stop a powerful industrialist from unleashing a weapon that could kill all mutants. A hyper-violent tour through some of its title antihero’s classic comics, Wolverine doesn’t quite reach the same heights as Spider-Man, but the way it lets players truly feel like “The Best There Is” is gold.

If you could rewind to your last day of school, what would it look like? Who are you with? And most importantly, what’s on the stereo? At the start of summer, developers Beethoven & Dinosaur released the gorgeous narrative game Mixtape, a loving homage to coming-of-age cinema with a tastefully curated soundtrack. (We’re talking Joy Division, Silverchair, B.J. Thomas, and more.) Set in the summer of 1995, the game sees three California teens relish their final hours together before going their separate ways. While Mixtape kicked up a storm among gamers over what defines a “game”—you can’t die or fail, nor are any of the things you do in Mixtape remotely challenging—it’s still a special experience that I argue illuminates how special games can be as a storytelling medium. Your adolescence probably didn’t look exactly like Mixtape, but the game can nonetheless trigger old memories.

As the world waits with bated breath to see who will play the next James Bond on the big screen, video games have a Bond to call their own in actor Patrick Gibson. From developer IO Interactive, of the smash Hitman series, comes 007 First Light, a high-octane espionage action game that follows a new James Bond in the early days of his career at MI6. Unlike the Hitman games, which are mired in the darkness of Agent 47’s deadly profession, 007 First Light revels in the fantasy of Bond’s perilous and pleasurable lifestyle. It’s basically the closest any of us will get to feeling like James Bond short of renting an Aston Martin for a test drive.

It’s been 12 years since Yacht Club Games released Shovel Knight, which was deemed by many to be one of the leading retro-inspired indie hits of the 2010s. Now Yacht Club has done it again with Mina the Hollower, which mines plenty of inspiration from gothic dungeon crawlers like Castlevania and Game Boy–era Legend of Zelda games, with modern flourishes à la Dark Souls. Players control Mina, a genius 19th-century mouse who must slay monsters and restore power to her home. Don’t let its cute looks fool you: Mina the Hollower is a challenging game that will have you sweating and smiling all the way through.

Remember dropping quarters into Marvel vs. Capcom 2 back in the day? The legacy of Marvel’s 2D-fighting heritage lives on in Marvel Tōkon: Fighting Souls, a shiny new team-based fighter with an anime-inspired take on the Marvel universe. Heroes like Captain America, Iron Man, Spider-Man, Storm, Deadpool, and Blade—and even villains like Doctor Doom—team up to save the world from an alien menace. The game is developed by Arc System Works, arguably the modern masters of the genre, boasting titles like Guilty Gear, BlazBlue, and Dragon Ball FighterZ, and the studio applies its decades of expertise to one of the finest and most nuanced fighting games we’ve ever had the pleasure of throwing our controllers at.

There’s no shortage of killer samurai games nowadays. Which is perhaps why Capcom saw fit to resurrect its dormant Onimusha franchise with the exceptional Onimusha: Way of the Sword. Essentially a reboot of the series (whose last title was released all the way back in 2006 for the PlayStation 2), Way of the Sword follows legendary swordsman Miyamoto Musashi as he comes into possession of the Oni Gauntlet and must cleanse the world of a supernatural evil. While not quite a Souls-like, the game offers an adequately complex combat system that ensures sword slashing never gets stale. And slicing demons in half feels oh so satisfying.

If you, like me, have fond memories of Initial D, Forza Horizon 6 is an invitation to awaken the sleeping gearhead within. The open-world racing series finally makes its way to Japan, the birthplace of drift racing, to let players roam dense city streets and wide-open mountain roads in tricked-out Hondas, Nissans, and exotic supercars. The Forza Horizon series has reached its peak with this sixth installment, with photorealistic graphics that can fool you into believing you’re actually speeding through traffic halfway around the world.

After almost 30 years of Resident Evil games, it’s no surprise that the ninth installment is another polished thrill ride. Capcom plays the hits to great success in Resident Evil Requiem, a survival-horror game that hits the right notes even when it’s serving up Leon S. Kennedy fan service. But in the roughly 10-to-18-hour campaign (a great length!), the gameplay of FBI newbie Grace Ashcroft is more horror focused and full of fresh twists on the Resident Evil formula. Though we won’t spoil it here, Esquire’s review in February also teased that the latest Resident Evil game contains “one major set piece in particular that rivals some of the best action-movie moments in recent years.”

Don’t let Pokémon Pokopia’s cozy gameplay fool you—the latest spin-off of the cutesy monster-collecting phenomenon is actually one of the deepest games in the franchise’s nearly thirty-year history. Sure, you will spend most of your time in Pokopia building houses for your Pokémon to live in or meticulously gardening. But underneath the casual novelties of building a perfect little Pokémon town lives a surprisingly emotional story about climate-change disasters, grief, and the importance of taking care of Earth’s natural environment.

Saros is a gameplay-first, story-second action game that feels incredible. Much as with the developer’s previous game, Returnal, the third-person shooting is inspired by arcade shooters where bullets fill your screen nonstop. Unlike Returnal, it’s not brutally difficult. Gameplay is fast and exhilarating—and sometimes overwhelming—but it doesn’t take long before you know exactly which projectiles to absorb, which to dodge, and which to parry. The PlayStation-exclusive title also has one of the best gameplay loops of the year, and it does more with the DualSense controller’s advanced haptic feedback than any other game on the Sony system. Merging the run-based progression of Hades with a more traditional level-based structure to enormous success, Saros is difficult yet approachable, rewarding players with a ton of resources to invest in upgrades as it slowly unfurls its mystery-box story bit by bit.

With the release of No More Heroes III in 2021, Grasshopper Manufacture finally put the saga of Travis Touchdown to rest and kicked off its next era with Romeo Is a Dead Man. The studio’s latest game retells Shakespeare’s famous love story mashed up with 1980s sci-fi, Japanese tokusatsu, and grind-house cinema. The game puts players in control of Romeo Stargazer, a small-town deputy who falls in love with an amnesiac named Juliet. But when Romeo dies, he is reborn as a half-man, half-machine superhero who embarks on a quest to find his missing love. Romeo Is a Dead Man feels like immersing oneself in an obscure Troma film, and it’s as madcap and magnificent as it sounds.

Remastered video games are always a tough sell—though with every Nintendo fan having no issue coughing up $30 to play Pokémon FireRed & LeafGreen on the Switch 2 this year, it seems times have changed. Luckily Dragon Quest VII Reimagined is much more than a port to a newer system. The remake, which includes many quality-of-life improvements, was rebuilt from the ground up with glorious 3D animation. If you’ve never played the classic Japanese RPG before, the charm of this latest remake is enough to make you a Dragon Quest fan for life.

If you’re too scared to play horror games alone, let Reanimal’s multiplayer gameplay bring people together. Set in an island town overrun with monsters and mysterious creatures, Reanimal follows a nameless brother and sister who must survive the island’s horrors if they want to save their friends. With a story as cryptic as some of its puzzles, Reanimal carries the baton of other memorable platformers like Limbo and Little Nightmares to deliver an otherworldly experience. Although Reanimal can be played alone, it is a journey best taken with someone by your side.

Video games are all about rewarding the player for progression, and there may be no better equivalent to this feeling in the real world than rock climbing. Cairn, a new indie title that focuses on this extreme sport, captures the similarities beautifully. As the player works their way up the mountain, juggling the character’s stamina and gauging the next best foothold, it is amazing how intense and immersive Cairn feels compared with its relatively beautiful visuals. But as with any good climb, the surprisingly difficult journey is more than worth it when you finally reach the top.

If you thought Doom had too many pixelated demons and not enough rubber-hose whimsy, Mouse: P.I. for Hire is your game. Heavily inspired by classic Disney, Fleischer, and MGM cartoons—and clearly taking some cues from the 2017 hit Cuphead—Mouse: P.I. for Hire puts players in control of an anthropomorphic mouse detective on his latest case in Mouseburg. The game’s fast-paced first-person-shooter action is delightfully complemented by its (literally) cartoonish antics. Hand-drawn pistol and shotgun blasts and reloading animations are as much a treat to watch as the game is fun to play. If gaming today feels more complicated than ever, Mouse: P.I. for Hire brings it back to basics with a novelty coat of paint.

Nintendo should have called the new Mario Tennis game Mario Tennis Chaos, because the player needs to understand right up front that skills on the court can carry them only so far in Mario Tennis Fever. Much like the 24 drivers per race in Mario Kart World, a calamity of new power-ups renders tennis into a zany, uncontrollable farce of a sport that becomes one of the best Mario sports titles in Nintendo’s history—once you learn to let go and embrace the nonsense.

The decades-old Marvel vs. Capcom series still reigns supreme as the best superhero tag fighter in video games, but Invincible VS is set on taking the throne. Based on the hit Amazon series (with some of its stars reprising their voice roles, like Oscar-winner J.K. Simmons as Omni-Man), Invincible VS is a 3v3 tag-team fighting game featuring playable characters from Robert Kirkman’s comic-book universe. The game tells a stand-alone story adjacent to season 3 in which Mark must stand with his allies to save Earth from the Viltrumites. (Hence the fighting.) Although its mechanical controls are relatively simple compared with those of other fighting games, it’s no less elegant and fun, and the game features some gnarly cel-shaded 3D animation that’s simply gorgeous. Besides, it’s another way to pummel your buddies to a bloody pulp. Talk about threatening us with a good time.

Dead Space? More like Dad Space. Pragmata is Capcom’s latest original game and a potential franchise in the making, its story centered around a systems engineer named Hugh and his foster-father relationship with an adorable android nicknamed Diana. With a rogue AI overtaking a research station on the moon, Hugh and Diana must work together to take the AI offline and make it back to Earth. Between its breakneck gameplay involving grid-like puzzles (which must be solved on the fly) and its endearing story about parenthood, Pragmata is what you might get if you asked ChatGPT, “What if Alien and Marvel’s Logan could be a single video game?”

#6891. Prince Harry Shocks Viewers With ‘This Morning’ Appearance—Here’s What He Revealed

Prince Harry has surprised This Morning viewers with an extra-long appearance on the most famous sofa in Britain. The Duke of Sussex appeared alongside regular Friday hosts Dermot O’Leary and Alison Hammond and joined them for a few segments throughout the morning show.

Over the course of his appearance on the ITV chat show, Harry—who was there to discuss his work with the Invictus Games—revealed more insight into his children Lilibet and Archie’s new life in the UK and gave a hint at his upcoming Christmas plans with Meghan and their family.

Here’s everything Prince Harry said throughout his appearance on This Morning.

Following the family’s move back to the UK in September earlier this year, Harry shared that the children, Lilibet and Archie, are finally getting British accents having spent most of their childhood so far in California, adding he was “squeezing the accents out of them.”

He also revealed there was a bit of a “culture shift” for the children when it came to their new schooling as they now have to wear school uniform, something they didn’t have to do while in school in the U.S.

“I think the kids being in school uniforms is a big change for them,” he explained. “But it has certainly helped. The confidence in both of them has gone through the roof since being in a UK school, which is really, really nice to see.”

During his chat with Alison and Dermot, the hosts quizzed him on all things UK vs. USA, and Alison asked him for his thoughts on Robert Pattinson being cast as James Bond, to which Prince Harry responded, “I think it’s in great hands.” He also added he couldn’t wait to see the film.

He was also asked whether he preferred EastEnders or Suits (which is the show his wife Meghan Markle is most known for acting in) and he ultimately picked Suits.

Every Friday on This Morning, hosts Dermot and Alison pick a variety of music to play throughout the show, and Harry was also given the chance to pick a song for Friday.

He revealed his choice was “Runnin’ (Lose It All)” by Naughty Boy feat. Beyoncé and Arrow Benjamin, and added it’s the song he listens to while doing a cold plunge.

The Duke of Sussex joined Dermot and Alison for the cooking segment of the series where they were learning how to create a Christmas cake with Phil Vickery.

During the segment, the hosts asked him about whether he liked to bake and he revealed that children Lilibet and Archie liked to bake, but that the whole family was a fan of The Great British Bake Off, which is of course presented by Alison.

Harry added that the series was good this year and he was a big fan of Tom to win the series.

As the cooking segment wound down, Alison asked him who would be doing the cooking at Christmas in his home this year.

He went on to say his wife and mother-in-law were the better cooks in his household and so they would be doing the cooking for the festive season. The Duke also revealed they may do things differently this year and have a Christmas gumbo, too!

#6887. Is The Rings of Power Coming Back for Season 4?

There’s One Ring to rule them all, but two seasons still to go.

There is One Ring to rule them all, but there are still two more seasons to go. The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power on Prime Video is fast approaching the end of its third season, which means some fans are now asking what the future has in store for the prequel series. While there aren’t definitive answers just yet, Tolkien fans can rest assured in knowing that a fourth season is more likely to happen than not. Here’s what we know so far.

On November 25, The Rings of Power will release the final two episodes of season 3. So, is season 4 a shoo-in? Almost. Per The Hollywood Reporter in a May 2026 story, preproduction was, at the time, “eyeing a schedule that includes preproduction on a fourth season this fall, with shooting to begin in early 2027.” As far as anyone outside the production knows, this work for season 4 has been underway.

In 2017, Amazon secured the rights to produce a Lord of the Rings television series for an eye-watering price tag of $250 million. In 2018, The Hollywood Reporter wrote that negotiations between Amazon and the Tolkien estate included a five-season commitment, while Amazon maintains authority to greenlight or cancel the series at any time. In October 2025, The Ankler reported that Tolkien has a $20 million per season “kill fee” if Amazon ends the show before its fifth season.

As of right now, Amazon has not formally greenlit The Rings of Power for season 4. But some signs point to it as inevitable. Amazon has not released viewership numbers for the current season, so only Amazon knows how the show is performing on Prime Video. Whether the audience is strong enough to keep the show going or Amazon simply wants to avoid kill fees (and a negative reputation in the industry for failing to finish a pricey Lord of the Rings show), will decide the future. For now, fans should enjoy the sunrise in Rivendell while there is still one.

#6886. Prince William and Kate Middleton’s Advisor Shares a Rare Comment About Their Relationship

A senior royal aide says the Prince and Princess of Wales are “very much a team” after their whirlwind India tour.

The Prince and Princess of Wales made headlines this week on their royal visit to India, which saw them attend the 2026 Earthshot Prize Awards ceremony, meet the country’s prime minister, drive a tuk-tuk, and even ride the metro. Now that they’re back in the UK, those in the couple’s inner circle have been reflecting on the trip, and one thing is clear: William and Kate are closer than ever.

Speaking to reporters (via the BBC), the pair’s most senior advisor described them as “very much a team” following their multiday tour in India. “It has been particularly special for them to experience this moment together,” said private secretary Ian Patrick. “They are very much a team.”

Pointing to the reason why, the royal insider explained that it’s all to do with taking part in each other’s passions. “The prince has really enjoyed having the princess alongside him for Earthshot,” the royal advisor said, referring to William’s environmental awards project. “She’s enormously proud of what he’s achieved.”

Commenting on William and Kate’s trip more broadly, the private secretary added, “The prince and princess have had a thoroughly enjoyable time in India. They’ve been incredibly touched by the warmth of the welcome they’ve received.”

Over the past few days, the Waleses have kept fans up to date with their adventures in India by sharing various photos and video clips on Instagram. In one down-to-earth video, William joined locals for a game of gully cricket in Mumbai. Another clip showed the pair wearing traditional South Asian style clothing at the Earthshot Prize Awards ceremony.

“The most beautiful and sweetest couple,” one fan commented on the video. “The most beautiful royal couple on the planet without a doubt,” agreed someone else.

We can’t wait to see where William and Kate’s next trip takes them!

#6885. The Breakfast at Tiffany’s NYC Townhouse Sells for $11.5 Million

The historic residence traded at a $3.5 million discount.

Holly Golightly might have had questionable taste in men, but she had a keen eye for real estate. Memorably portrayed by Audrey Hepburn in Breakfast at Tiffany’s, Golightly lived in a handsome Upper East Side brownstone that had been subdivided into apartments. Though the 1961 classic film shot its interior scenes on a Los Angeles soundstage, the exterior of the New York City townhouse served as a backdrop throughout the movie.

In May 2025, the five-story residence hit the market for $15 million, following a total overhaul that included a slew of luxe upgrades, such as a new roof terrace and an elevator. Although StreetEasy listed the property as “in contract” last December, its official sale date is November 11, 2026. In the end, the two-family house traded hands for $11.5 million, roughly 23 percent off the original price, per Realtor.com.

Set on a tree-lined block between Lexington and Third Avenues, the East 73rd Street home is distinguished by its Tiffany Blue front door and matching window casings. Built in 1866 by architect John Sexton, the Italianate-style landmark encompasses nearly 4,500 square feet and has four bedrooms in total, three in the main residence—which spans the top four floors—and one in the garden-level apartment, which has its own separate entrance.

The seller was retired beverage executive Joseph Harkins, who acquired the property in 2015 for $7.4 million and spearheaded a complete transformation that modernized the interiors while still preserving the house’s 19th-century charm. The project even included excavating the basement level, which now has nearly eight-foot ceilings, a powder room, a second laundry room, and a wine cellar.

Outside, the 470-square-foot rear garden is fully enclosed by Walpole lattice fencing and sports stonework paving. It will no doubt be the scene of plenty of “Breakfasts” for the home’s newest steward.

Geoffrey Montes is an associate editor at ELLE Decor with a serious love for all things real estate and design. Before that, he worked at Architectural Digest, Galerie, and Preservation magazines, covering everything from jaw-dropping listings to world-famous architects and design events like Salone del Mobile and Homo Faber.

#6884. Wait, Did Hailey Bieber Buy CBK’s Jacket on the RealReal?

Well, no, but there’s a lot to unpack here

Yesterday, Hailey Bieber took her obsession with ’90s minimalism one step further with a reference to the ultimate ‘90s minimalist: Carolyn Bessette-Kennedy. For a talk at the 2026 DealBook Summit here in New York, she and her stylist Dani Michelle pulled a Yohji Yamamoto look from Spring 1998, the one with a black, high-necked jacket with a line of delicate buttons paired with a white linen column skirt so famously worn by CBK in June 1998. It did what it was supposed to do: Stop us in our scrolling. But it jogged something else in the collective consciousness of fashion lovers everywhere. Hadn’t this jacket just made the rounds on our feeds?

A few weeks ago, savvy shoppers spotted the look for sale on the RealReal. Bessette-Kennedy’s reverence for Yohji Yamamoto is one that fashion’s acolytes tend to share, so it didn’t take long for the listing to go viral. The description identified its starry provenance: “From the Spring/Summer 1998 Collection; as worn by Carolyn Bessette-Kennedy in 1998 and worn by Winona Ryder for the season 5 premiere of 'Stranger Things' in 2025.”

TRR listed the jacket for $7,025. It sat on the site long enough to make the Instagram rounds, but someone snatched it up before the site had time to mark it down. The jacket sold for full price on November 6, less than two weeks before the DealBook Summit.

Could it be that Bieber scrolls for vintage Yohji on the RealReal like the rest of us? Does she have saved searches? Is she watching her obsessions with the eyes of a hawk? (Or has she outsourced this job to Michelle or someone on her team?) Bazaar reached out to the RealReal, and a spokesperson revealed that while all customer data is private, they know that Bieber’s look did not come from them. Well, it was fun to imagine. But just the possibility that this could have happened is enough to make you think.

Michelle’s post of the look does not reveal her source, though days later, she confirmed the source to be Tab Vintage. The vintage resale platform Shy mentioned in comment sections that it recently sold the same piece. Another one of the jackets, also without a proven link to CBK’s personal wardrobe, sold via the Fashion Auctioneer in March for $12,500. That’s the thing with ready-to-wear: It’s not one-of-a-kind. Bessette-Kennedy bought her jacket off the rack, which means there are multiple versions floating around out there.

If you scroll down the jacket’s sold page on the RealReal to the “Related Items” section, you’ll find multiple listings of the Cruise 2005 Chanel jacket Andy Sachs famously wore in The Devil Wears Prada, priced at roughly ten grand. “Similar style seen on Anne Hathaway in 'The Devil Wears Prada,'” the description reads. “We are seeing more and more archival pieces with incredible cultural provenance come in on consignment,” says Kristen Naiman, chief brand officer at the RealReal. “And we love helping these shards of fashion history resurface in pop culture.”

It’s become common practice for major stylists to mine specialty vintage stores for these megawatt, nostalgia-driven red carpet references. Just look at Law Roach’s Instagram account, where he documents pulling 1950s Balenciaga for Kim Kardashian from Lily et Cie, a source that specializes in museum-quality vintage. But as this thought experiment exemplifies, these transactions are becoming more normalized.

Naiman continues: “Whether you know Carolyn Bessette-Kennedy or not, there is now an entire new generation coveting this Yohji Yamamoto look on Hailey Bieber, and it has a renewed desirability because of that. And that’s the fun of being a fashion hobbyist and TRR enthusiast. You not only love the piece, you know the love story behind the piece. It’s a way to participate in fashion history. You can spend years coveting a jacket that has left an indelible mark on culture and then, one day, find it in your size.”

Which brings us back to that Yohji jacket that sold on November 6. We know Bieber wasn’t the one who purchased it, but some lucky person did, and now they own a piece of fashion history. Wonder how many other people have the same one.

#6882. The Car Coat Is Back and Cooler Than Ever

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Looking to score one for yourself? We rounded up top-notch options from Dunhill, Percival, and more.

You don’t necessarily need a car coat. If you’re in the market for a new coat and not, like us, learned in the world of 20th century menswear, it just might be one of the few styles you’ve heard the word for—peacoat and trench coat likely being two others. All three are great if you’re looking for something a bit grown up than your average off-duty jacket. A trench coat would give you gravitas, a peacoat versatility; the car coat, though, strikes a nice middle ground. Find the right one, and it can replace 90 percent of your other coats.

At its inception, the car coat was a heavy outer layer. Cars were still open to the elements, so the rich who owned them needed floor-length fur jackets to keep themselves warm as they went motoring and did a lap around the neighborhood on Christmas. As cars became more more enclosed and more part of daily life, the car coat became more practical. The length got shorter—eventually settling around mid-thigh—to be comfortable when you sit in the driver seat but with enough coverage to be considered a coat. Materials shifted away from furs to leather and suede, then to even lighter-weight gabardines and weather-resistant materials. They

But current iterations of the car coat don’t exactly follow a formula. As we’ve seen with Harringtons, brands just start to use the most widely-known phrasing to get guys into buying a coat. We, in the interest of keeping some amount of historical accuracy, will stick to a formula. A car coat has that mid-thigh length, a turn down collar, and a practicality for someone getting in and out of a seat. They’re great during fall, and they can be worn through winter if you live somewhere that doesn’t get consistently below freezing. Here, you’ll find our favorite picks.

We’re starting on the high end, but it’s only because if you’re looking for a by-the-book garment or accessory tied to motoring history, you go to Dunhill. In its original form, Alfred Dunhill’s company sold motoring accessories. Overcoats, goggles, picnic sets, horns, lamps, dashboard clocks—the tagline was “Everything but the motor.” It was a luxury men’s brand for the premier luxury hobby of the time.

The company has gone through several iterations, but has always maintained a bend towards that luxury man. Now, under the creative direction of Simon Holloway, it’s a standard-bearer for a slightly more fashion-forward vision British menswear. Take this car coat for example. It calls back to the house’s original business model, and it is classic in its finishes: turn down collar, natural shoulders, cuff straps, and totally un-adorned through the torso. But the fabric is a gorgeous wool, silk, and cashmere blend. It’s a genius balance between tradition and fashion, one that only Dunhill is well-positioned to put out.

Length: mid-thigh Material: 84% wool, 13% silk, 3% cashmere

Now that we’ve gotten the formalities out of the way, let’s talk value. For the money, Percival is the best on this list. Hell, it is really just the best on this list overall.

The fabric is the same you’d find on a trench—a tight-weave polyester and cotton blend. The cut is simple and easy to wear. The length, crucially, is more suited to the modern driving experience. There’s a bit of bum coverage, but if you were out running errands and getting in and out of the car, you can keep the coat on. We also love the classic colors and patterns the team cut this in.

All of that for an attainable price, it’s one of the best items in the Percival lineup.

Length: just over your rearMaterial: 65% polyester, 35% cotton

Short of finding an Etsy seller, this is probably the closest you’ll get to the genuine car coats of the ‘50 and ‘60s. The fabric flutters just around your knees just like they did way back when, while a classic collar and navy blue hue adds to the retro nostalgia. Made of 100% cotton, the fabric is finished with C0 water repellency and lined with cotton to give the fashion even more function. We’d recommend wearing and styling like you would a trench (though, if you want something more substantial, J.Crew also makes the Rivington in cozy wool for those who in search of a new winter coat.)

Length: mid-thigh, grazes the kneeMaterial: 100% cotton

If you liked all the above, but didn’t totally love the over $500 price tag—check out H&M. Similar silhouette and weight. Different material and color.

Length: just a little below the kneeMaterial: Polyester 94%, Rayon 5%, Spandex 1%

This one doesn’t need much explaining. Imagine the iconic Burberry trench, cut to a more modest car coat shape.

This bridges a little more into topcoat territory, but its mid-thigh length balances it back into a car coat classification. Still, if you live in a more temperate climate and are in the market for a more substantial cold weather layer, look no further.
